\put(10.0,20.0){$T_{v_{1}}$}
\put(60.0,20.0){$T_{v_{2}}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 19
.
Collapsing an interior edge of a
5
-leafed tree
T
(top). The metric ribbon subtrees associated to
v_{1},v_{2}\in\Ve(T)
(bottom).

Definition 7.17
.

A smooth section
\phi_{T}=(\phi_{T,v})_{v\in\Ve(T)}
of
\mathcal{V}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}
is called
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}
, if the metric ribbon tree induced by
\phi_{T,v,r}
is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}_{v}
(in the sense of Definition
7.10
) for all
r\in\mathcal{R}_{v}
, and additionally,

\Res_{f}(\phi_{T,v,r})=\mathfrak{w}(f)^{2}.

for all flags
f
attached to
v
. In this case, one can choose a set of
\phi_{T}
-adapted strip-like ends
\iota_{T}=(\iota_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
for the family
\mathcal{S}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}
, that is, a set of proper embeddings,

\iota_{f}:\mathbb{R}_{t}^{\pm}\times[0,\pi\mathfrak{w}(f)]_{s}\times\mathcal{R}_{v}\to\mathcal{S}_{v},

fibered over
\mathcal{R}_{v}
, one for each flag
f
attached to
v
, and which is
\phi_{T,v,r}
-adapted on each fiber
S_{v,r}
(cf. Section
7.3
). The width of each
\iota_{f}
is
\mathfrak{w}(f)
. In particular,
\iota_{f}^{*}(\phi_{T,v,r})
is the standard
(2,0)
-tensor
dz\otimes dz,z=t+is
on the semi-finite strip
\mathbb{R}_{t}^{\pm}\times[0,\pi\mathfrak{w}(f)]_{s}
.
∎

For any smooth section
\phi_{T}=(\phi_{T,v})_{v\in\Ve(T)}
of
\mathcal{V}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}
and any pair
T^{\prime}>T
, consider the gluing map

\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}:(-1,0)^{\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}\times\mathcal{R}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}},

defined using a set of
\phi_{T}
-adapted strip-like ends. We briefly recall the construction below. Similar to (
7.13
), for any
(r_{v})\in\mathcal{R}^{T}
and
(\rho_{e})\in(-1,0)^{\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}
, the truncated surface associated to
r_{*}=\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}((\rho_{e}),(r_{v}))\in\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}}
is obtained by gluing
S_{v,r_{v}}^{\trun},v\in\Ve(T)
with some strips, one for each interior edge in
T
but not in
T^{\prime}
, along the vertical boundary:

(7.16)

\displaystyle S_{r_{*}}^{\trun}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\coprod_{v\in\Ve(T)}S_{v,r_{v}}^{\trun}\coprod_{e\in\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}\Stp_{e}/\sim,

\displaystyle\Stp_{e}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[0,-\mathfrak{w}(e)\ln(-\rho_{e})]_{t}\times[0,\pi\mathfrak{w}(e)]_{s}

where
\iota_{f^{+}(e)}(0,s)
is identified with
(0,s)\in\Stp_{e}
, and
\iota_{f^{-}(e)}(0,s)
with
(-\ln(-\rho_{e}),s)\in\Stp_{e},s\in[0,\pi\mathfrak{w}(e)]_{s}
. This time the width of each
\Stp_{e}
is dictated by the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
. Finally, we attach semi-infinite strips, one for each component of
\partial^{v}S^{\trun}_{r_{*}}
, to obtain the union of pointed disks that corresponds to
r_{*}
.

The upshot is that in the meantime one may glue quadratic differentials
\phi_{T}
to obtain a section of
\mathcal{V}^{T^{\prime}}
on the image of
\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}
: the quadratic differential on the fiber of
r_{*}=\overline{\gamma}^{T,T_{*}}((\rho_{e}),(r_{v}))
is defined by patching
\phi_{T,v,r_{v}}
on
S^{\trun}_{v,r_{v}}
with the standard
(2,0)
-tensor
dz\otimes dz
on all
\Stp_{e}
and on semi-infinite strips under the decomposition (
7.16
). This quadratic differential is holomorphic, since the strip-like ends are adapted to
\phi_{T}
(in other words, we are gluing singular flat surfaces; see Figure
23
in the next section for an illustration). A smooth
\phi=(\phi_{T})
of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{d+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}
is called
consistent
if for any
T<T^{\prime}
,
\phi^{T^{\prime}}
is the section obtained by gluing
\phi_{T}
on the image of
\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}
.

In order to construct the Fukaya-Seidel categories, there is one final requirement for
\phi=(\phi_{T})
: families with the same set of labels are considered the same, and so they come with the same family of quadratic differentials and strip-like ends. This means that to any subset
A\subset\partial\mathcal{T}
is associated a smooth section
\psi_{A}
of
\mathcal{V}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
, and for any
T
and
v\in\Ve(T)
, we require that
\phi_{T,v}=\psi_{A}
if
\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}=A
, i.e., if the family
\mathcal{S}_{v}\to\mathcal{R}_{v}
is labeled by
A
. We insist a similar condition for the set of strip-like ends. More concretely, one can prove:

Lemma 7.18
.

For
\epsilon>0
and any metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
with
(d+1)
exterior vertices, one can construct inductively for any subset
A\subset\partial\mathcal{T}
with
|A|\geq 3
,

•

a smooth section
\psi_{A}
of
\mathcal{V}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
that is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}_{A}
(in the sense of Definition of
7.17
);

•

a set of
\psi_{A}
-adapted strip-like ends
(\iota_{A,k})_{k=0}^{|A|-1}
for the family
\mathcal{S}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
;

which defines for each
d
-leafed tree
T
,

•

a smooth section
\phi_{T}=(\phi_{T,v})_{v\in\Ve(T)}
of
\mathcal{V}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}
;

•

a set of
\phi_{T}
-adapted strip-like ends
\iota_{T}=(\iota_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
for the family
\mathcal{S}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}
;

and which satisfies the following properties:

(i)

\phi_{T,v}=\psi_{A}
and
\iota_{f_{k}(v)}=\iota_{A,k},0\leq k\leq|v|-1,
if
A=\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}
;

(ii)

\phi=(\phi_{T})
is a smooth section of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{d+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}
;

(iii)

for any pair
T<T^{\prime}
,
\phi_{T^{\prime}}
is obtained by gluing
\phi_{T}
on the image of
\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}
;

(iv)

\{(\iota_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T)}\}_{T}
is a consistent choice of strip-like ends in the sense of
[
Sei08
, Lemma 9.3]
.

In this case, we say that
\phi=(\phi_{T})
is a
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T})
-consistent section of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{d+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}
, and that
(\iota_{T})
is a set of strip-like ends adapted to
(\phi_{T})
.

Remark 7.19
.

By the proof of
[
Sei08
, Lemma 9.3]
, the last property

(

⁢
i
v

)

implies that the following diagram is commutative for any
T<T^{\prime}<T^{\prime\prime}
:

(7.17)

Proof of Lemma
7.18
.

The argument is similar to that of
[
Sei08
, Lemma 9.3]
. For any
3\leq n\leq d+1
, let
0<\epsilon_{n}\ll\epsilon
be a small constant to be fixed in the proof. For any
A\subset\partial\mathcal{T}
, we shall construct a
(\epsilon_{|A|},\mathcal{T}_{A})
-consistent family of quadratic differentials and a set of strip-like ends adapted to this family over
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{|A|}
using induction. When
|A|=3
,
\mathcal{R}^{3}
is a single point, and a compatible quadratic differential is determined uniquely by its complex residues; see Example
7.8
; so the statement holds trivially, and we can take
\epsilon_{3}
as small as we wish.

The induction then proceeds on
n=|A|
, and without loss of generality, one may focus on the last step when
n=d+1
, as this explains the general case as well. Hence, we may assume that
\phi_{T}=(\phi_{T,v})
and
(\iota_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
have been constructed for all
T<T_{*}
which satisfy the consistence conditions.

The section
\phi_{T_{*}}=\psi_{\partial\mathcal{T}}
on the top stratum
\mathcal{R}^{T_{*}}=\mathcal{R}^{d+1}\subset\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}
is determined by the third property

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

on the image of
\gamma^{T,T_{*}}
, which is smooth by the test (
7.15
). By induction hypothesis, each
\phi_{T,v}
is
\epsilon_{|v|}
-close to the subtree
\mathcal{T}_{v}
. This implies that
\phi_{T_{*}}
is at least
\epsilon_{T}^{*}
-close to
\mathcal{T}
on the image of
\gamma^{T,T_{*}}
with

(7.18)

\epsilon_{T}^{*}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}10\sum_{v\in\Ve(T)}\epsilon_{|v|}

(this follows again from an inductive argument on
|\Ve(T)|
). The commutativity of (
7.17
) (with
T^{\prime\prime}=T_{*}
) then implies that the section
\phi_{T_{*}}
would agree on the overlapped region. Set

(7.19)

\epsilon_{d+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\max_{T<T_{*}}\epsilon_{T}^{*}.

By Lemma
7.14
,
\phi_{T_{*}}
can be extended to a smooth section over all
\mathcal{R}^{d+1}
which is still
\epsilon_{d+1}
-close to
\mathcal{T}
. In a similar vein, the gluing construction determines the strip-like ends
(\iota_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T_{*})}
near the boundary of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}
; cf.
[
Sei08
, Lemma 9.3]
. An extension of
\phi_{T_{*}}
-adapted strip-like ends over
\mathcal{R}^{d+1}
clearly exists.

Finally, the constant
\epsilon_{n}
is determined recursively by (
7.18
) and (
7.19
), which depends only on the possible degenerations of a
(n+1)
-leafed tree (which is dependent of the metric ribbon subtree
\mathcal{T}_{A}
). To make the section
\phi_{T}

\epsilon
-close to the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
for all
T
, it suffices to choose
\epsilon_{3}\ll 1
sufficiently small to start. This completes the proof of Lemma
7.18
.
∎

8.
Fukaya-Seidel Categories

8.1.
Introduction

Let
(q_{j})_{j=0}^{n}
be a collection of critical points of
W:M\to\mathbb{C}
, not necessarily distinct. A set
\mathbf{Th}=(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q_{1},\theta_{1}},\cdots,\Lambda_{q_{n},\theta_{n}})
of thimbles is called
admissible
if

•

\theta_{n}<\cdots<\theta_{1}<\theta_{0}<\theta_{n}+2\pi
;

•

for all
0\leq j\leq n
, the projection
l_{q_{j},\theta_{j}}=W(\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}})
does not contain any critical values of
W
other than its end point.

\begin{overpic}[Pic39.png]
\put(27.0,3.0){\small$W(q_{0})$}
\put(58.0,3.0){\small$W(q_{1})$}
\put(70.0,29.0){\small$W(q_{2})$}
\put(44.0,42.0){\small$W(q_{3})$}
\put(12.0,29.0){\small$W(q_{4})$}
\put(100.0,17.0){\small$=\text{ a base point}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 20
.
An admissible set of thimbles.

The set
\mathbf{Th}
is ordered such that
\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}}\prec\Lambda_{q_{k},\theta_{k}}
if
\theta_{k}<\theta_{j}
. For convenience, write
\Lambda_{j}=\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}}
. Choose admissible Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{jk}
, one for each pair
(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k}),\ j<k
. In this section, we shall construct a directed
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
with
\Ob\mathcal{E}=\mathbf{Th}
and whose morphism spaces are defined by the formula

(8.1)

\hom_{\mathcal{E}}(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{ if }j>k,\\
\mathbb{F}\cdot e_{\Lambda_{j}}&\text{ if }j=k,\\
\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk})&\text{ if }j<k,\end{array}\right.

where the Floer complex
\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk})
is defined as in Section
2
. We shall verify that
\mathcal{E}
is well-defined up to canonical quasi-isomorphisms, meaning that the
A_{\infty}
-categories
\mathcal{E}
defined using different choices of auxiliary data in the construction or different Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{jk}
are all quasi-isomorphic, and the quasi-isomorphism is unique up to homotopy. The most relevant examples for our applications are the following.

Example 8.1
.

Define the set of
critical angles
of
(M,W)
as

\mathscr{C}(M,W)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\frac{W(q_{0})-W(q_{1})}{|W(q_{0})-W(q_{1})|}:q_{0},q_{1}\in\Crit(W),W(q_{0})\neq W(q_{1})\}\subset S^{1}.

Any
\theta_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
is called
admissible
if
e^{i\theta_{*}}\not\in\mathscr{C}(M,W)
. Denote by
\theta_{*}^{\crit}
the smallest angle greater than
\theta_{*}
such that
e^{i\theta_{*}^{\crit}}
is critical. Thus any angles in
[\theta_{*},\theta_{*}^{\crit})
are still admissible, and the critical values
\im(e^{-i\theta_{*}}W(q)),q\in\Crit(W)
are mutually distinct. Label the set
\Crit(W)=\{y_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
such that

(8.2)

\im(e^{-i\theta_{*}}W(y_{1}))<\im(e^{-i\theta_{*}}W(y_{2}))<\cdots<\im(e^{-i\theta_{*}}W(y_{m})),

and choose angles
\theta_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq m
such that

(8.3)

\theta_{m}<\cdots<\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}\subset(\theta_{*},\theta_{*}^{\crit}).

Then the set
\mathbf{Th}_{\theta_{*}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\Lambda_{y_{1},\theta_{1}},\cdots,\Lambda_{y_{m},\theta_{m}})
is admissible. In this case,
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\theta_{*}})
is called
the Fukaya-Seidel category
of the Landau-Ginzburg model
(M,W)
in the direction of
\theta_{*}
. By Proposition
8.24
below,
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\theta_{*}})
is independent of the choice of
(\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{m})
satisfying (
8.3
) up to canonical quasi-isomorphisms.

It is sometimes convenient to choose a base point
w_{\theta_{*}}\in\mathbb{C}
far away in the direction of
e^{i\theta_{*}}
and ask all rays
l_{y_{j},\theta_{j}},1\leq j\leq m
to pass through
w_{\theta_{*}}
; see Figure
21
below. In particular,
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\theta_{*}})
will be independent of the choice of
w_{\theta_{*}}
.
∎

Example 8.2
.

For convenience, we shall always assume in this paper that
\theta_{*}=0
is admissible, and so is
\theta_{*}=\pi
. Let
\theta_{\star}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0^{\crit}
. A thimble
\Lambda_{q,\theta},\ q\in\Crit(W)
is called
stable
if
\theta\in(0,\theta_{\star})
and respectively
unstable
if
\theta\in(\pi,\pi+\theta_{\star})
. Choose angles such that

(8.4)

0<\theta_{m}<\cdots<\theta_{1}<\theta_{0}<\theta_{\star}<\pi<\eta_{0}<\eta_{1}<\cdots<\eta_{m}<\pi+\theta_{\star},

and define

\displaystyle\mathbf{Th}_{0}

\displaystyle=(S_{1},S_{2},\cdots,S_{m}),

\displaystyle S_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Lambda_{x_{j},\theta_{j}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}

\displaystyle=(U_{m},U_{m-1},\cdots,U_{1}),

\displaystyle U_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Lambda_{x_{j},\eta_{j}},\ 1\leq j\leq m.

We insist that
\Crit(W)=\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
is ordered as in the set
\mathbf{Th}_{0}
, so

(8.5)

H(x_{1})<H(x_{1})<\cdots<H(x_{m}).

Then
\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{0})
is called the Fukaya-Seidel category of
(M,W)
, and
\mathcal{B}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi})
the Koszul-dual category of
\mathcal{A}
.

The union
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}
is also admissible, which ordered by

(8.6)

U_{m}\prec U_{m-1}\prec\cdots U_{1}\prec S_{1}\prec S_{2}\prec\cdots\prec S_{m}.

Following the convention in Section
6.2
, the directed
A_{\infty}
category
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0})
defines a diagonal
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
-bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}
which assigns to each pair
(U_{j},S_{k})
the Floer complex
\Ch^{*}_{\natural}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{*})
. By Lemma
5.9
and Lemma
5.10
, for some good choices of Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{*}
,

(8.7)

\Delta(U_{j},S_{k})=\Ch^{*}_{\natural}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{*})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{ if }j\neq k,\\
\mathbb{K}&\text{ if }j=k.\end{array}\right.

Thus the induced functor
r_{\Delta}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{Q}_{r}=\rfmod(\mathcal{B})
identifies
\Ob\mathcal{A}
with
\Ob\mathcal{B}^{!}
. The Koszul duality between
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
is verified by the following theorem, whose proof is accomplished in Section
10
.
∎

Theorem 8.3
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{k}\in\Ob\mathcal{Q}_{r},1\leq k\leq m
denote the image of
S_{k}
under
r_{\Delta}
. Then for all
1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq m
, the chain map

(8.8)

(r_{\Delta})^{1}:\hom_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{k_{1}},S_{k_{2}})\to\hom_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}(\mathcal{S}_{k_{1}},\mathcal{S}_{k_{2}})

is a quasi-isomorphism. Thus the
A_{\infty}
-functor
r_{\Delta}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{Q}_{r}
is a quasi-isomorphism onto its image, and
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
form a Koszul duality pair in the sense of Definition
6.10
.

Remark 8.4
.

By Proposition
8.24
,
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0})
is well-defined up to canonical quasi-isomorphism. Then by Lemma
6.3
, it suffices to verify (
8.8
) for some convenient choices of Floer data. If
\mathfrak{a}^{*}_{jk}
are chosen so that (
8.7
) holds, then (
8.8
) is easily verified for all
k_{1}\geq k_{2}
, because
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}^{!}
are both directed. Thus the challenging part of Theorem
8.3
is for
k_{1}<k_{2}
.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

The construction of
\Delta
can be generalized slightly to the following scenario: let
\mathbf{Th}_{st}
(resp.
\mathbf{Th}_{un}
) be any admissible set of stable (resp. unstable) thimbles, and set
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{st})
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{un})
. Then
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{un}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{st})
defines a
(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\mathcal{B}})
-bimodule
\widetilde{\Delta}
. This point of view will become useful in the proof of Theorem
8.3
.
∎

\begin{overpic}[Pic40.png]
\put(46.0,52.0){\small$W(x_{4})$} \put(46.0,43.0){\small$W(x_{3})$} \put(46.0,28.0){\small$W(x_{2})$} \put(46.0,20.0){\small$W(x_{1})$}
\put(80.0,48.0){\small$H(x_{4})$} \put(80.0,40.0){\small$H(x_{3})$} \put(80.0,32.0){\small$H(x_{2})$} \put(80.0,24.0){\small$H(x_{1})$} \put(85.0,55.0){\small$w_{0}$} \put(18.0,13.0){\small$w_{\pi}$} \put(100.0,72.0){\small$S_{1}$}
\put(100.0,68.0){\small$S_{2}$} \put(100.0,64.0){\small$S_{3}$} \put(100.0,60.0){\small$S_{4}$} \put(-3.0,0.0){\small$U_{4}$}
\put(-3.0,5.0){\small$U_{3}$} \put(-3.0,10.0){\small$U_{2}$} \put(-3.0,15.0){\small$U_{1}$} \end{overpic}

Figure 21
.
The diagonal bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Remark 8.6
.

There is a rotational operation
\mathcal{R}
on the space of admissible sets of thimbles which moves the first thimble to the last one. This means that for any
\mathbf{Th}=(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\cdots,\Lambda_{q_{n},\theta_{n}})
,
\mathcal{R}\mathbf{Th}
is defined by

(\Lambda_{q_{1},\theta_{1}},\cdots,\Lambda_{q_{n},\theta_{n}},\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}-2\pi}),

so the set of angles becomes
\theta_{0}-2\pi<\theta_{n}<\cdots<\theta_{1}
. By Lemma
5.11
, the canonical isomorphism

\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}},\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}-2\pi})\cong D\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}})

is grading-preserving. Thus
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{R}\mathbf{Th})=\mathcal{C}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
if one ignores the degree shifting in the definition of
\mathcal{C}
in (
6.15
). To some extent, only the cyclic ordering on
\mathbf{Th}
is essential to this construction.
∎

8.2.
Cobordism data and moduli spaces for families

The rest of Section
8
is devoted to the construction of
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
. From now on fix an admissible set of thimbles

\mathbf{Th}=(\Lambda_{0},\cdots,\Lambda_{n}),\ \Lambda_{j}=\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}},\ 0\leq j\leq n,\ n\geq 2,

along with a metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
with
(n+1)
exterior vertices. We shall think of
\mathbf{Th}
as a set of labels for the universal family
\mathcal{S}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
by identifying

(8.9)

\mathbf{Th}\cong\partial\mathcal{T}=\{\mathfrak{o}_{0},\cdots,\mathfrak{o}_{n}\},\ \Lambda_{j}\mapsto\mathfrak{o}_{j}

as ordered sets. Let
\mathfrak{w}_{jk}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}d_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathfrak{o}_{j},\mathfrak{o}_{k}),\ 0\leq j<k\leq n
denote the distance of
\mathfrak{o}_{j},\mathfrak{o}_{k}
in
\mathcal{T}
, and choose any admissible Floer data

\mathfrak{a}_{jk}=(\mathfrak{w}_{jk},\alpha_{jk}(s),\beta_{jk},\epsilon_{jk},\delta H_{jk}),

one for each pair
(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k}),\ j<k
. Thus for some
0<\delta\ll 1
we must have

\displaystyle\alpha_{jk}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{k}&\text{ if }s\geq\mathfrak{w}_{jk}-\delta,\\
\theta_{j}-\pi&\text{ if }s\leq\delta,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\re(e^{i(\beta_{jk}-\alpha_{jk}(s)})>\epsilon_{jk}\ \forall s\in\mathbb{R}_{s},

and the perturbation 1-form
\delta H_{jk}
is supported on
[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s}\subset\mathbb{R}_{s}
. For simplicity, write
\mathfrak{a}_{0}=(\mathfrak{w}_{0},\alpha_{0}(s),\beta_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta H_{0})
for
\mathfrak{a}_{0n}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{k}=(\mathfrak{w}_{0},\alpha_{k}(s),\beta_{k},\epsilon_{k},\delta H_{k})
for
\mathfrak{a}_{(k-1)k},1\leq k\leq n
. Let
\gamma_{k}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}
be a characteristic curve of
\alpha_{k}(s)
. To construct the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
, it suffices to take
\mathcal{T}
to be the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}^{n+1}
in Example
7.8
, in which case
\mathfrak{w}_{jk}=\pi
for all
j<k
. But for future reference we shall develop the theory for a general
\mathcal{T}
as well.

Let
S
be any
(n+1)
-pointed disk labeled by
\mathbf{Th}
and equipped with an
S
-compatible quadratic differential
\phi
. The completion of
(S,\phi)

\hat{S}=S\ \bigcup\ \mathbb{H}^{-}_{0}\cup\cdots\cup\mathbb{H}^{-}_{n},

carries a complete Riemannian metric. Fix a set of strip-like ends
(\iota_{k})_{k=0}^{n}
adapted to
\phi
. Assume that
\phi
is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}
for some
\epsilon>0
and the complex residues of
\phi
are fixed by
\mathcal{T}
; see Section
7.2
for relevant notations.

The Floer equation (
5.5
) on this completion is specified by a phase pair
(\Xi,\delta\kappa)
, whose construction will follow closely the case for continuation maps; see Section
5.2
. Note that the inclusion map
\sigma_{k}:\mathbb{H}^{+}_{k}\to\hat{S}
extends to an isometric embedding
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times(-\infty,\delta]\to\hat{S}
for some
0<\delta\ll 1
. For any
K>0
, we consider smooth embeddings

\Xi^{\dagger}:S\to\mathbb{C}

that satisfy the following properties:

•

for some constant
c_{\Xi^{\dagger}}^{k}\in\mathbb{C},1\leq k\leq n
, we have

(8.10)

\displaystyle\Xi^{\dagger}\circ\iota_{0}(t,s)

\displaystyle=-i\epsilon_{0}e^{i\beta_{0}}\cdot t+\gamma_{0}(s)

\displaystyle\text{ on }\mathbb{R}^{-}_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{0}]_{s},

\displaystyle\Xi^{\dagger}\circ\iota_{k}(t,s)

\displaystyle=-i\epsilon_{k}e^{i\beta_{k}}\cdot t+\gamma_{k}(s)+c^{k}_{\Xi^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle\text{ on }[K,+\infty)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{k}]_{s};

•

for some
0<\delta\ll 1
,

(8.11)

\Xi^{\dagger}\circ\sigma_{k}(t,s)=g_{k}(t)+e^{i\theta_{k}}\cdot s\text{ on }\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[0,\delta]_{s},

where
g_{k}(t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Xi^{\dagger}\circ\sigma_{k}(t,0)
;

•

for any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}
and
0\leq k\leq n
,

(8.12)

-\im(e^{-i\theta_{k}}\partial_{t}g_{k}(t))-\frac{1}{2}|\partial_{t}g_{k}(t))|^{2}>0.

Denote by
\Emb_{K}^{S}
the space of such embeddings and by

S^{\trun}_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}S\big\backslash\bigg(\iota_{0}\big((-\infty,0)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{0}]_{s}\big)\coprod_{k=1}^{n}\iota_{k}\big((K,+\infty)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{k}]_{s}\big)\bigg)

the truncation of
S
. Because any embedding
\Xi^{\dagger}
is determined by its restriction on
S^{\trun}_{K}
,
\Emb_{K}^{S}
is identified with a subspace of
C^{\infty}(S^{\trun}_{K};\mathbb{C})
and equipped with the smooth topology. For any
K<K^{\prime}
, the inclusion map
\Emb_{K}^{S}\to\Emb_{K^{\prime}}^{S}
is continuous.

Lemma 8.7
.

The direct limit
\Emb^{S}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim\Emb^{S}_{K}
is weakly contractible.

Sketch of Proof.

The proof follows the same line of arguments as in Lemma
5.6
, and we only give a sketch. Let
\Emb_{K}^{S,*}
denote the space of embeddings
\Xi^{\dagger}:S\to\mathbb{C}
satisfying (
8.10
) and a weaker version of (
8.12
):

(8.13)

-\im(e^{-i\theta_{k}}\partial_{t}g_{k}(t))>0,\ \forall t\in\mathbb{R}_{t},\ 0\leq k\leq n.

The second space
\Emb_{K}^{S,*}
is easier to work with for two reasons: 1) it depends only on the underlying smooth surface of
S
(not on the metric); 2) it is invariant under self-diffeomorphisms of
S
fixing the complement
S\setminus S^{\trun}_{K}
. For any continuous map
\varphi:S^{n}\to\Emb^{S}_{K}
, the null-homotopy of
\varphi
in
\Emb^{S}
is constructed in three steps, using
\Emb_{K}^{S,*}
as an intermediate space:
i
) by taking
c^{k}_{\Xi^{\dagger}}=0
,
ii
) by pushing
\Xi^{\dagger}
“outwards” along the boundary, and
iii
) by applying a family of self-diffeomorphisms of
S
. At each step, one may take the freedom to increase
K
; cf. the proof of Lemma
5.6
. We leave the details to interested readers.
∎
