\end{overpic}

Figure 22
.
The phase function
\Xi
on
\hat{S}
. Shaded regions denote the image of
\Xi\circ\hat{\iota}_{k}
when either
t\leq 0
or
t\geq K
. Red curves represent the images of
g_{k}(t)
. This figure should be compared with Figure
15
.

By (
8.11
), any
\Xi^{\dagger}\in\Emb_{K}
extends to an orientation-reversing diffeomorphism
\Xi:\hat{S}\to\mathbb{C}^{2}
by setting

(8.14)

\Xi\circ\sigma_{k}(t,s)=g_{k}(t)+e^{i\theta_{k}}\cdot s\text{ on }\mathbb{R}_{t}\times(-\infty,\delta],0\leq k\leq n

and
\Xi=\Xi^{\dagger}
on
S
. Away from
S^{\trun}_{K}
, the smooth 1-form
\delta\kappa\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{S};\mathbb{C})
is defined by the formulae

(8.15)

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}^{*}(\delta\kappa)

\displaystyle=\im(\overline{\partial_{t}g_{k}(t)}\cdot W),0\leq k\leq n,

\displaystyle\text{ on }\mathbb{H}^{-}_{k},

\displaystyle\hat{\iota}_{0}^{*}(\delta\kappa)

\displaystyle=\im(i\epsilon_{0}e^{-i\beta_{0}}dt\cdot W),

\displaystyle\text{ on }\mathbb{R}^{-}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s},

\displaystyle\hat{\iota}_{k}^{*}(\delta\kappa)

\displaystyle=\im(i\epsilon_{k}e^{-i\beta_{k}}dt\cdot W),1\leq k\leq n,

\displaystyle\text{ on }[K,+\infty)_{s}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}.

Remark 8.8
.

By (
8.10
) and (
8.15
), the phase pair
(\Xi,\delta\kappa)
agrees with the canonical one associated to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
on the incoming plane-like end
\hat{\iota}_{0}
and with the one associated to
\mathfrak{a}_{k}
on
\hat{\iota}_{k}([K,+\infty]_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
up to a translation by the constant
c_{\Xi^{\dagger}}^{k}\in\mathbb{C}
. In particular, the phase function
\Xi
takes the form (see Figure
22
and compare (
8.10
))

\displaystyle\Xi\circ\hat{\iota}_{0}(t,s)

\displaystyle=-i\epsilon_{0}e^{i\beta_{0}}\cdot t+\gamma_{0}(s),

\displaystyle\text{ on }\mathbb{R}_{t}^{-}\times\mathbb{R}_{s},

\displaystyle\Xi\circ\iota_{k}(t,s)

\displaystyle=-i\epsilon_{k}e^{i\beta_{k}}\cdot t+\gamma_{k}(s)+c^{k}_{\Xi^{\dagger}},\ 1\leq k\leq n,

\displaystyle\text{ on }[K,+\infty)_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}.\qed

Definition 8.9
.

Given an admissible Floer datum
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
for the pair
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{n})
and
\mathfrak{a}_{k}
for each
(\Lambda_{k-1},\Lambda_{k}),1\leq k\leq n
, a cobordism datum on
(S,\phi)
is a quadruple
\mathfrak{b}_{\phi}=(K,\Xi,\delta\kappa,\delta H)
where

•

K>0
and the phase pair
(\Xi,\delta\kappa)
is constructed using an embedding
\Xi^{\dagger}\in\Emb_{K}^{S}
such that for some
\epsilon_{S}>0
,

(8.16)

-\det D\Xi-|\delta\kappa^{0,1}|^{2}>\epsilon_{S}\text{ on }\hat{S};

•

the perturbation 1-form
\delta H\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{S},\mathcal{H})
is supported on
S
and for any
0\leq k\leq n
,
\iota_{k}^{*}\delta H=\delta H_{k}
on the strip-like end.

By Remark
8.8
and (
8.12
) , the pointwise estimate (
8.16
) holds for some
\epsilon_{S}>0
away from the truncated surface
S^{\trun}_{K}
, and (
8.16
) will hold on
\hat{S}
for a possibly smaller
\epsilon_{S}
if we extend
\delta\kappa
by the zero form over
S^{\trun}_{K}
. Thus the space of cobordism data is nonempty.
∎

Remark 8.10
.

One should think of
\mathfrak{b}_{\phi}
as associated to the singular flat surface
(S,\phi)
not just to the smooth surface
S
. A cobordism datum encodes the information to define the Floer equation as well as the way to write the energy estimate.
∎

The Floer equation (
5.5
) associated to any cobordism datum
\mathfrak{b}_{\phi}
recovers the
\alpha_{k}
-instanton equation on each plane-like end
\hat{\iota}_{k}
and takes the standard form

\partial_{t}P+J(\partial_{s}P+\nabla\re(e^{-i(\theta_{k}-\pi)}W)\circ P)=0,\ 0\leq k\leq n

on each planar end
\mathbb{H}^{-}_{k}
. Now choose an
\alpha_{k}
-soliton, one for each marked point
\zeta_{k}
of
S
,

\displaystyle p_{0}(s)

\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{C}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{n};\mathfrak{a}_{0}),

\displaystyle p_{k}(s)

\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{C}(\Lambda_{k-1},\Lambda_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{k}),\ 1\leq k\leq n,

The boundary condition for a solution of (
5.5
) is specified by a model map
P_{\model}:\hat{S}\to M
such that
P_{\model}\circ\iota_{k}(t,s)=p_{k}(s)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{\pm}_{t}
(i.e., it is time-independent) and such that for all
0\leq k\leq n
,

P_{\model}\circ\sigma_{k}(t,s)\to q_{k}\text{ as }s\to-\infty.

This decay is understood as in (
2.13
) and is required to be exponential and uniform in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}
. The moduli space

(8.17)

\mathcal{M}_{S}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\phi})

then consists of all solutions of (
5.5
) on
\hat{S}
which have finite
L^{2}_{k}
-distance to
P_{\model}
for all
k\geq 2
. The excision argument in the proof of Proposition
4.2
reduces the index computation of
\mathcal{M}_{S}
to the case of Lagrangian boundary condition. Then
[
Sei08
, Proposition 11.13]
implies that

(8.18)

\text{the expected dimension of }\mathcal{M}_{S}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\phi})=\gr(p_{0})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\gr(p_{k}).

To define the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
, we have to consider the family version of (
8.17
) when
S
is allowed to vary in the universal family
\mathcal{S}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
. From now on let
\phi
be a smooth section of
\mathcal{V}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
which is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}
; then
\phi
determines a smooth fiber bundle

\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1},

whose fiber at any
r\in\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
is the completion
\hat{S}_{r}
of
(S_{r},\phi_{r})
.

Definition 8.11
.

Given any admissible Floer datum
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
for the pair
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{n})
and
\mathfrak{a}_{k}
for each
(\Lambda_{k-1},\Lambda_{k}),1\leq k\leq n
, a cobordism datum for the family
\mathcal{S}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
labeled by
\mathbf{Th}
and equipped with the section
\phi
is a quadruple
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}=(K_{\mathbf{Th}},\Xi_{\mathbf{Th}},\delta\kappa_{\mathbf{Th}},\delta H_{\mathbf{Th}})
consisting of

•

a constant
K_{\mathbf{Th}}>0
and a smooth function
\Xi_{\mathbf{Th}}\in C^{\infty}(\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1};\mathbb{C})
;

•

relative 1-forms
\delta\kappa_{\mathbf{Th}}\in\Omega^{1}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}/\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}(\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1};\mathbb{C})
and
\delta H_{\mathbf{Th}}\in\Omega^{1}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}/\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}(\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1};\mathcal{H})

such that
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
restricts to a cobordism data
\mathfrak{b}_{\phi_{r}}=(K_{\mathbf{Th}},\Xi_{r},\delta\kappa_{r},\delta H_{r})
on each fiber
(S_{r},\phi_{r}),\ r\in\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
, and (
8.16
) holds for a uniform constant
\epsilon_{\mathbf{Th}}>0
independent of
r
.
∎

The cobordism datum
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
is called
admissible
if for every
(n+1)
-tuple
\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n}
, the parametrized moduli space

(8.19)

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\coprod_{r\in\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}\mathcal{M}_{S_{r}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\phi_{r}}),

is cut out transversely. This means that the extended linearized operator is surjective at any point of (
8.19
); see for instance
[
Sei03
, Section (9h)]
.

8.3.
Choosing cobordism data consistently

From now on fix an
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T})
-consistent section
\phi
for the bundle
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
and a set of strip-like ends
(\iota_{T})
adapted to
\phi=(\phi_{T})
in the sense of Lemma
7.18
.

The construction of Section
8.2
can be carried out also for any subset
A\subset\mathbf{Th}
with
|A|\geq 3
. Denote by
\mathcal{T}_{A}\subset\mathcal{T}
the unique metric ribbon subtree with
\partial\mathcal{T}_{A}\cong A
under the map (
8.9
). A component of
\phi=(\phi_{T})
and
(\iota_{T})
then defines a smooth section
\psi_{A}
of

\mathcal{V}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|},

which is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}_{A}
, together with a set of strip-like ends
(\iota_{A,k})_{k=0}^{|A|-1}
adapted to
\psi_{A}
. Choose any admissible cobordism datum
\mathfrak{b}_{A}=(K_{A},\Xi_{A},\delta\kappa_{A},\delta H_{A})
for the section
\psi_{A}
and the family
\mathcal{S}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
labeled by
A
, then one can form a parametrized moduli space similar to (
8.19
).

For any
n
-leafed tree
T
and
v\in\Ve(T)
, write
\mathfrak{b}_{v}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{b}_{\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}}
for the cobordism datum on the family
\mathcal{S}_{v}\to\mathcal{R}_{v}
, which is labeled by
\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}\subset\mathbf{Th}
, and
K_{v}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K_{\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}}>0
for the first component of
\mathfrak{b}_{v}
. For any
T<T^{\prime}
, the gluing construction in Section
7.8
applied to
(\mathfrak{b}_{v})_{v\in\Ve(T)}
defines a cobordism datum on the image

\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}(-e^{-K_{v^{+}(e)}},0)\times\mathcal{R}^{T}\bigg)\subset\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}}.

We shall only sketch this construction for the codimension-1 stratum and when
T^{\prime}=T_{*}
; the general case follows from a similar scheme. Let
e\in\Ed^{\inte}(T)
denote the unique interior edge of
T
. Suppose that
e
is adjacent to
\mathfrak{o}_{j},\mathfrak{o}_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Write
v^{\pm}=v^{\pm}(e)
,
f^{\pm}=f^{\pm}(e)=(v^{\pm},e)
and
K_{\pm}=K_{v^{\pm}}
. For any
r_{\pm}\in\mathcal{R}_{v^{\pm}}
and
\rho_{e}\in(-e^{-K_{+}},0)
, there is a composition map

(8.20)

\Emb_{K_{+}}^{S_{r_{+}}}\times\Emb_{K_{-}}^{S_{r_{-}}}\to\Emb_{\max\{K_{+},K_{-}\}}^{S_{r_{*}}}.

with
r_{*}=\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}(\rho_{e},(r_{\pm}))
. Recall that
S_{r_{*}}
is obtained by gluing

S_{r_{+}}\setminus\iota_{f^{+}}\big((l_{e},+\infty)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s}\big)\text{ with }S_{r_{-}}\setminus\iota_{f^{-}}\big((-\infty,0)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s}\big),\ l_{e}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-\ln(-\rho_{e}),

where
\iota_{f^{+}}(l_{e},s)
is identified with
\iota_{f^{-}}(0,s),s\in[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s}
. Then for any
\Xi^{\dagger}_{\pm}\in\Emb_{K_{\pm}}^{S_{r_{\pm}}}
, the image of
(\Xi^{\dagger}_{+},\Xi^{\dagger}_{-})
under (
8.20
) is defined by the formula

(8.21)

\Xi^{\dagger}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Xi^{\dagger}_{+}(z)&z\in S_{r_{+}}\setminus\iota_{f^{+}}\big((l_{e},+\infty)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s}\big),\\
\Xi^{\dagger}_{-}(z)+c_{\Xi^{\dagger}_{+}}^{f^{+}}+(-i\epsilon_{jk}e^{i\beta_{jk}})\cdot l_{e}&z\in S_{r_{-}}\setminus\iota_{f^{-}}\big((-\infty,0)_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s}\big).\end{array}\right.

Remark 8.12
.

Roughly speaking, (
8.21
) is saying that the phase function is normalized such that on the unique incoming end it agrees with the canonical phase function associated to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
(see Example
5.4
), and
\Xi^{\dagger}_{-}
is translated to match the function
\Xi^{\dagger}_{+}
on an outgoing end of
S_{r_{+}}
.

The composition map (
8.20
) is defined only when
l_{e}\geq K_{+}
, or equivalently,
\rho_{e}\geq-e^{-K_{+}}
, and it allows us to obtain a phase function on
\hat{S}_{r_{*}}
from the ones on
\hat{S}_{r_{\pm}}
. One should compare (
8.21
) with the composition map (
5.19
) in the case of continuation maps.

It is slightly easier to think of this gluing construction in terms of
\kappa_{\Xi}
, the induced 1-form of
\Xi
defined by (
5.3
). The primary reason to work with
\Xi
is that one has to verify that the space of the cobordism data is weakly contractible, which requires that the connection
d+\kappa_{\Xi}
be negatively curved all the time. Using phase functions proves extremely useful in this regard — it allows us to prove Lemma
8.7
using the contractibility
[
Sma59
]
of
\Diff(D^{2},\partial D^{2})
.
∎

The correction 1-form
\delta\kappa
(and resp.
\delta H
) can be glued in a straightforward manner, as it is already set to equal on

\displaystyle\iota_{f^{+}}([K_{+},l_{e}]_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s})\subset S_{r_{+}}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\iota_{f^{-}}([K_{+}-l_{e},0]_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{jk}]_{s})\subset S_{r_{-}}.

For any
A\subset\mathbf{Th}
with
|A|\geq 3
, choose an admissible cobordism data
\mathfrak{b}_{A}
. Then this family
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}
is called
consistent
if for all
A
,
\mathfrak{b}_{A}
agrees with the cobordism datum obtained by this gluing construction in a neighborhood of the boundary of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{|A|}
. For instance, when
A=\mathbf{Th}
, this means that for any
d
-leafed tree,
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
is obtained by gluing
(\mathfrak{b}_{v})_{v\in\Ve(T)}
on the image

\gamma^{T,T_{*}}\bigg((-\delta,0)^{\Ed^{\inte(T)}}\times\mathcal{R}^{T}\bigg)\subset\mathcal{R}^{n+1}

for some
0<\delta\ll 1
.

Lemma 8.13
.

A consistent family
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}
of cobordism data exists.

Proof of Lemma
8.13
.

The proof follows the same line of arguments as in Lemma
7.18
. The induction starts with the trivial case when
|A|=3
, where no consistency conditions are required. For the induction step, we focus on the last step when
A=\mathbf{Th}
, in which case the cobordism data obtained by the gluing construction near different
T
-strata of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
all match up and therefore determines
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
in a neighborhood of the boundary of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
. The remaining question is to extend
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
over the whole family
\mathcal{S}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
; in particular, one has to find a phase function

\Xi_{\mathbf{Th}}:\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}\to\mathbb{C}

extending a given one defined near the boundary of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
. By construction, each
\Xi_{r}:\hat{S}_{r}\to\mathbb{C}
is determined by an embedding
\Xi_{r}^{\dagger}:S_{r}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfying the conditions (
8.10
)(
8.11
)(
8.12
).

Topologically,
(\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1},\partial\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1})
is homeomorphic to
(D^{n-2},S^{n-3})
, and
\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
is identified with the interior of
D^{n-2}
. We are in the same situation as in Lemma
8.7
, except that the conditions (
8.11
) and (
8.12
) are stated for a family of
(n+1)
-pointed disks instead of a single one. However, the space
\Emb_{K}^{S,*}
introduced in the proof of Lemma
8.7
depends only on the underlying smooth surface of
S
. Following the proof of Lemma
5.6
, one may first construct an extension in
\Emb_{K_{\mathbf{Th}}}^{S,*}
for some
K_{\mathbf{Th}}\gg 1
and then apply a family of self-diffeomorphisms of the domain to achieve (
8.11
) and (
8.12
) for each fiber
S_{r}
.

The extension of
\delta\kappa_{\mathbf{Th}}
and
\delta H_{\mathbf{Th}}
is much more straightforward. Finally,
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
is admissible if the extension of
\delta H_{\mathbf{Th}}
is chosen generically.
∎

Remark 8.14
.

As we shall never consider the Floer cohomology of a thimble with itself, our consistency condition is slightly more rigid (and more convenient) than the one in
[
Sei08
, Lemma 9.5]
.
∎

8.4.
The energy estimate and compactness

Given a consistent family of cobordism data
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}
associated to
\mathbf{Th}
, we have to verify that the parametrized moduli space (
8.19
) is compact when its expected dimension is zero. This is guaranteed by the energy estimate from Lemma
5.2
combined with the argument in Section
3
. For future reference, we record this estimate as follows:

Lemma 8.15

(The Energy Estimate III)
.

For any
(n+1)
-pointed disk
(S,\phi)
equipped with a compatible differential and any cobordism datum
\mathfrak{b}_{\phi}=(K,\Xi,\delta\kappa,\delta H)
, consider the truncated surface

\hat{S}_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\hat{S}\setminus\bigg(\hat{\iota}_{0}(\{t<-K\})\coprod_{k=1}^{n}\hat{\iota}_{k}(\{t>K\})\bigg).

For any solution
P\in\mathcal{M}_{S}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{S})
, let

\tilde{p}_{0}(s)=P\circ\hat{\iota}_{0}(-K,s)\text{ and }\tilde{p}_{k}(s)=P\circ\hat{\iota}_{k}(K,s),s\in\mathbb{R}_{s},1\leq k\leq d,

denote the restrictions of
P
on certain time slices. Then the energy of
P
on the truncation
\hat{S}_{K}
can be bounded as follows

(8.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(p_{0})

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(p_{k})+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\im(W(q_{k})\cdot\overline{g_{k}(t_{k}^{+}+K)-g_{k}(-K)})

\displaystyle\geq(\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(p_{0})-\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(\tilde{p}_{0}))+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(\tilde{p}_{k})-\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(p_{k}))+\epsilon_{S}^{*}\int_{\hat{S}_{K}}u,

where
\epsilon_{S}^{*}
is independent of
P
and
t_{k}^{+}=t^{+}_{k}(\phi),0\leq k\leq n
is the length of the
k
-th boundary component
C_{k}
as defined in (
7.2
). Each term on the right hand side of (
8.22
) is non-negative and therefore is bounded above by the left hand side of (
8.22
).

Proof.

Apply Lemma
5.2
to the surface
\hat{S}_{K}
to derive that

(8.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(\tilde{p}_{0})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(\tilde{p}_{k})+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\im(W(q_{k})\cdot\overline{g_{k}(t_{k}^{+}+K)-g_{k}(-K)})\geq\epsilon_{S}^{*}\int_{\hat{S}_{K}}u,

which is (
8.22
). On the plane-like end
\hat{\iota}_{0}((-\infty,-K]\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
, the solution
P
is a downward gradient flowline of
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
, and so

\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(p_{0})\geq\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(\tilde{p}_{0}).

Similarly,
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(\tilde{p}_{k})\geq\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(p_{k}),1\leq k\leq d
.
∎

By (
8.12
),
|\partial_{t}g_{k}(t)|<2
. Lemma
8.15
implies that the total drop of the action functional is bounded below by

(8.24)

\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(p_{0})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(p_{k})\geq-2\sum_{k=0}^{n}(2K+t^{+}_{k})|W(q_{k})|,

if the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{S}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{S})
is non-empty. The next lemma says that this lower bound can be made uniform for the parametrized moduli spaces.

Lemma 8.16
.

Given any consistent family of cobordism data
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}
, there exists a constant
C_{\mathbf{Th}}>0
depending on the phase functions such that if
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}})
is non-empty, then

(8.25)

\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(p_{0})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}}(p_{k})\geq-C_{\mathbf{Th}}.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for any
r\in\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
, if the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{S_{r}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\phi_{r}})
is non-empty, then (
8.25
) holds for some uniform constant of
C_{\mathbf{Th}}>0
. If
r
lies in a fixed compact subset of
\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
, then
(
8.25
)
follows from (
8.24
), since
K=K_{\mathbf{Th}}
is fixed and
t^{+}_{k}(S_{r},\phi_{r}),0\leq k\leq n
are bounded uniformly. Fix some
0<\delta\ll 1
. In general, for any
A\subset\mathbf{Th}
with
|A|\geq 3
, one can choose inductively an open subset
U_{A}\subset\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
with compact closure to obtain an open cover
(V_{T})
of
\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
as follows: let

V_{T}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\gamma^{T,T^{*}}\bigg((-\delta,0)^{\Ed^{\inte(T)}}\times U_{T}\bigg)\subset\mathcal{R}^{n+1},\ U_{T}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{v\in\Ve(T)}U_{\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}}\subset\mathcal{R}^{T},

for all
T<T^{*}
and
V_{T^{*}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U_{\mathbf{Th}}
. If
r\in V^{T}
for some
T<T^{*}
, then we write
r=\gamma^{T,T^{*}}((\rho_{e})_{e\in\Ed^{\inte(T)}},(r_{v})_{v\in\Ve(T)})
, and (
8.25
) is proved using the estimate (
8.23
) on each
S_{r_{v}},v\in\Ve(T)
and by noting that over each gluing region the solution
P
is a downward gradient flowline for an action functional, so when we add up (
8.23
) for all
S_{r_{v}},v\in\Ve(T)
, each interior edge of
T
contributes a negative term on the left hand side, which allows us to prove (
8.25
) for this
r
. The figure below illustrates the case when
n=3
.
∎

\begin{overpic}[Pic45.png]
\put(-10.0,10.0){\small$\tilde{p}_{0}(s)=\hat{\iota}_{0}(-K,s)$}
\put(35.0,3.0){\small$\tilde{p}_{1}(s)=\hat{\iota}_{1}(K,s)$}
\put(95.0,25.0){\small$\tilde{p}_{2}(s)=\hat{\iota}_{2}(K,s)$}
\put(95.0,52.0){\small$\tilde{p}_{3}(s)=\hat{\iota}_{3}(K,s)$}
\put(35.0,52.0){\small$\tilde{p}_{e}^{+}(s)=\hat{\iota}_{f^{+}(e)}(-\ln(\delta),s)$}
\put(55.0,25.0){\small$\tilde{p}_{e}^{-}(s)=\hat{\iota}_{f^{-}(e)}(-K,s)$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 23
.
The gluing region is shaded by red. The value of the action functional at
\tilde{p}^{+}_{e}
is always greater than that at
\tilde{p}^{-}_{e}
.

Remark 8.17
.

At the first sight, the estimate (
8.25
) does not seems very interesting as the
(n+1)
-tuple
\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n}
has only finitely many possibilities on the left hand side. The upshot is that this constant
C_{\mathbf{Th}}
does not even depend on
(M,W)
but only on the phase functions on
\mathcal{S}^{n+1}\to\mathcal{R}^{n+1}
. The proof of Lemma
8.16
will be used again when we construct the geometric filtration on the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}
in Section
9.4
; cf. Lemma
9.16
.
∎

8.5.
Construction

Given an
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T})
-consistent section
\phi=(\phi_{T})
of the bundle
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
, a set of strip-like ends
(\iota_{T})
adapted to
\phi
and a consistent family
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})
of cobordism data, the directed
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))
is constructed as follows. The morphism spaces are defined by (
8.1
). For any subset
A=(\Lambda_{j_{0}},\cdots,\Lambda_{j_{d}})\subset\mathbf{Th}
,
d\geq 2
, the
A_{\infty}
-operation

(8.26)

\mu_{\mathcal{E}}^{d}:\hom_{\mathcal{E}}(\Lambda_{j_{d-1}},\Lambda_{j_{d}})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hom_{\mathcal{E}}(\Lambda_{j_{0}},\Lambda_{j_{1}})\to\hom_{\mathcal{E}}(\Lambda_{j_{0}},\Lambda_{j_{d}})[2-d]

is defined by counting the
0
-dimensional parametrized moduli space over the family
\hat{S}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
, with the Floer equation (
5.5
) determined by the cobordism datum
\mathfrak{b}_{A}
. More concretely, when
A=\mathbf{Th}
, (
8.26
) is defined by the formula (see (
8.19
))

(8.27)

\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}p_{k}\mapsto\sum_{p_{0}:\dim\mathcal{M}=0}\#\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n};\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}})\cdot p_{0}.

The
A_{\infty}
-associativity relations (
6.1
) are then verified by the standard argument; cf.
[
Sei08
, Section 9]
.

8.6.
The cohomological category

Our next step is to understand the invariance of
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))
. When
n=2
,
\mathcal{R}^{3}
is a single point, and the quadratic differential
\phi
is uniquely determined by the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
by Example
7.8
. In this case, the only information carried by
\mathcal{E}
is the product map on the cohomological category:

[\mu^{2}_{\mathcal{E}}]:\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{12})\otimes\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1};\mathfrak{a}_{01})\to\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{02}).

The continuation method in Section
5.2
can be generalized further, using Lemma
8.7
instead of Lemma
5.6
, to show that
[\mu^{2}_{\mathcal{E}}]
is independent of the cobordism data
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
and is natural with respect to the continuation map (
5.9
). Note also that the completion
\hat{S}
of a (2+1)-pointed disk
S
is independent of the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
(though the metric may change on a compact subset); the different choices of
\mathcal{T}
only affect the inclusion map

\sigma_{k}:\mathbb{H}^{-}_{k}\to\hat{S},\ 0\leq k\leq 2

by a translation in the
s
-coordinate. In this case,
\mathcal{T}
is only used to determined the region of
\hat{S}
on which the phase pair
(\Xi,\delta\kappa)
takes the standard form (
8.14
)(
8.15
). We conclude that the product map

(8.28)

m_{*}=[\mu^{2}_{\mathcal{E}}]:\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})\otimes\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1})\to\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2}),

is canonically defined for any admissible triple
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
. This shows that for any admissible set
\mathbf{Th}
of thimbles the cohomological category of
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}})
is independent of
\mathcal{T},\ \phi
and
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})
and that (
8.28
) is associative.

8.7.
Quasi-units

For any
q\in\Crit(W)
and
\theta_{1}<\theta_{0}<\theta_{1}+2\pi
, let
e_{q}
denote the canonical generator of
\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q,\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q,\theta_{1}})
obtained in Lemma
5.10
. One major failure of our construction so far lies in the fact that the strict units of
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))
are defined formally, as we were unable to define the Floer cohomology of a thimble with itself. However, these generators
e_{q}
, which are called
quasi-units
, will partially remedy this failure, as they behave pretty much like isomorphisms in the cohomological category of
\mathcal{E}
, except that they do not admit an inverse. This will be fixed in Section
8.9
using categorical localization to establish the invariance of
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
.

Proposition 8.18
.

Given any admissible set
(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q_{1},\theta_{1}},\Lambda_{q_{2},\theta_{2}})
with
q_{1}=q_{2}=q\in\Crit(W)
, suppose that the ray
l_{q_{0},\theta_{0}}
interests with
l_{q,\theta_{j}}
at
y_{j}\in\mathbb{C},j=1,2
and that the triangle formed by
(y_{0},y_{1},W(q))
contains no critical values of
W
in the interior, then the product map

m_{*}(e_{q},\cdot):\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q,\theta_{1}})\to\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q,\theta_{2}}).

is an isomorphism. Similarly, if
q_{0}=q_{1}=q
,
l_{q_{2},\theta_{2}}
intersects with
l_{q,\theta_{j}}
at
y_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},j=0,1
and the triangle formed by
(y_{0}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime},W(q))
contains no critical values of
W
in the interior, then the map

m_{*}(\cdot,e_{q}):\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q,\theta_{1}},\Lambda_{q_{2},\theta_{2}})\to\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{q,\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q_{2},\theta_{2}}).

is an isomorphism.

\begin{overpic}[Pic11.png]
\put(-9.0,14.0){\small$q_{1}=q_{2}$} \put(0.0,1.0){\small$q_{0}$}
\put(32.0,14.0){\small$q_{2}$} \put(29.0,1.0){\small$q_{0}=q_{1}$} \put(62.0,4.0){\small$q_{0}=q_{1}=q_{2}$} \put(90.0,14.0){\small$e^{i\theta_{0}}$}
\put(96.0,8.0){\small$e^{i\theta_{1}}$}
\put(94.0,2.0){\small$e^{i\theta_{2}}$}
\put(18.0,12.0){\small$y_{1}$}
\put(14.0,4.0){\small$y_{2}$}
\put(50.0,3.0){\small$y_{1}^{\prime}$}
\put(47.0,11.0){\small$y_{0}^{\prime}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 24
.
Quasi-units.

Remark 8.19
.

The heuristic behind Proposition
8.18
is straightforward: as we vary the angle
\theta_{1}
continuously to deform the thimble
\Lambda_{q,\theta_{1}}
into
\Lambda_{q,\theta_{2}}
(in the first case), the Floer cohomology is unaffected if the triangle of interest contains no other critical values of
W
; otherwise, the angle
\theta_{1}
may come across a value for which the condition (
2.8
) is violated and the Floer cohomology is undefined.
∎

Proposition
8.18
will be proved in Section
11.6
using a vertical gluing theorem Here we content ourselves with a special case.

Lemma 8.20
.

For any admissible set
\mathbf{Th}=(\Lambda_{q_{0},\theta_{0}},\Lambda_{q_{1},\theta_{1}},\Lambda_{q_{2},\theta_{2}})
of thimbles with
q_{k}=q\in\Crit(W),\ 0\leq k\leq 2
, we have
m_{*}(e_{q},e_{q})=e_{q}
.

Proof of Lemma.

Let
S
be a (2+1)-pointed disk inducing
m_{*}
and
\mathcal{T}
a metric ribbon tree with
|\partial\mathcal{T}|=3
. Choose a collection of admissible Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{jk}
, one for each pair
(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k}),0\leq j<k\leq 2
. We take the perturbation 1-forms
\delta H
to be zero in
\mathfrak{a}_{jk}
and also in the cobordism datum
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}}
. Recall from Lemma
5.10
that in this case any
\alpha_{jk}
-soliton is a constant map at
q
with
\gr=0
. Let
P\in\mathcal{M}_{S}(p_{0},p_{1},p_{2};\mathfrak{b})
be any solution that contribute to the chain-level map (
8.27
) that defines
m_{*}
; then each
p_{k},0\leq k\leq 2
is constant at
q
. The energy estimate from Lemma
8.15
implies that
P
is also constant at
q
. The easiest to see this is to set
P\equiv q
to start, then the equality in (
8.22
) must hold (one has to go back to the proof of Lemma
5.2
and check that the equality indeed holds at each step). This forces the left hand side of (
8.22
) to vanish. Alternatively, one may simply repeat the proof of Lemma
5.10
.

Finally, we have to show that the linearized operator
\mathcal{D}
at the constant map
P\equiv q
is invertible, so the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{S}
is regular. As in the proof of Lemma
5.10
, we reduce this problem to the case of Example
2.2
and the same energy estimate then shows that
\mathcal{D}
is injective. By (
8.18
), the Fredholm index of
\mathcal{D}
is
\gr(e_{q})-\gr(e_{q})-\gr(e_{q})=0
, so
\mathcal{D}
is invertible.
∎

8.8.
A remark on the pair-of-pants coproduct

We add a short digression to discuss a coproduct structure on
H(\mathcal{E})
. In Section
8.6
, the operations
\mu_{\mathcal{E}}^{d},\ d\geq 2
are defined using phase functions
\Xi_{r}:\hat{S}_{r}\to\mathbb{C}
, whose mapping degree is equal to
-1
, but one may also consider phase functions of degree
k\in[-d,-1]
, which is modeled on a map

\hat{S}_{r}\xrightarrow{\text{degree }(-1)}\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{z\mapsto z^{k}}\mathbb{C}.

Although the pointwise estimate (
5.6
) will be violated at the zero locus of
\det D\Xi
, we still have
-\det D\Xi-|\delta\kappa^{0,1}|^{2}\geq 0
if
\delta\kappa\equiv 0
in a neighborhood of this locus; the proof of the compactness theorem then carries over with only minor differences. In particular, for any admissible
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
, one may construct a coproduct map:

\blacktriangle:\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2})\to\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})\otimes\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1}).

using a phase function of degree
(-2)
on the completion of an
(1+2)
-pointed disk
S^{\ddagger}
with
|\Sigma^{-}|=2
and
|\Sigma^{+}|
=1; see Figure
25
.

It is not our goal to incorporate this coproduct map in the present work, but we shall point out a notable difference of
\blacktriangle
with
m_{*}
: Lemma
8.20
is not true for
\blacktriangle
. If
q_{k}=q,\ 0\leq k\leq 2
and
\delta H\equiv 0
, the Floer equation (
5.5
) can only have a constant solution
P\equiv q
on
\hat{S}^{\ddagger}
, but this solution is not regular and the Fredholm index of the linearized operator
\mathcal{D}^{\ddagger}
is
-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}M
. Indeed, using the same excision principle as in Proposition
4.2
and
[
Sei08
, Proposition 11.13]
, we compute that

\Ind\mathcal{D}^{\ddagger}=n(1-|\Sigma^{-}|)+\gr(e_{q})+\gr(e_{q})-\gr(e_{q})=-n.

Thus the constant solution on
\hat{S}^{\ddagger}
is not regular and will disappear under a generic perturbation. This explains why
m_{*}
is the right operation to define the Fukaya-Seidel category of
(M,W)
.

\begin{overpic}[Pic12.png]
\put(50.0,29.0){$\hat{S}^{\ddagger}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 25
.
The coproduct map.

8.9.
Invariance

To prove that
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))
is independent of the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
turns out to be a subtle problem; to simplify our exposition, we fix the choice of
\mathcal{T}
to define the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
:

Proposition 8.21
.

For any admissible set
\mathbf{Th}
of thimbles with
n+1=|\mathbf{Th}|
, take
\mathcal{T}
to be the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}^{n+1}
in Example
7.8
. Then the directed
A_{\infty}
-category

\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T}^{n+1},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))

is independent of the choice of
\phi
and
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})
up to canonical quasi-isomorphisms.

The proof below is inspired by
[
GPS20
]
[
AS19
, Appendix A]
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma
denote the space of admissible sets
\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}=(\Lambda^{\prime}_{0},\cdots,\Lambda^{\prime}_{n}),\Lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime}},0\leq j\leq n
. Define a partial order
\xrightarrow{s}
on
\Gamma
as follows:
\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}
if

\theta_{n}^{\prime\prime}<\theta_{n}^{\prime}<\cdots<\theta_{1}^{\prime\prime}<\theta_{1}^{\prime}<\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}<\theta_{0}^{\prime},

and if the interval
[\theta_{j}^{\prime\prime},\theta_{j}^{\prime}]
consists of admissible angles for all
j
(in the sense of Example
8.1
). This means that the ray
l_{q_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime}}
can be continuously deformed into
l_{q_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime\prime}}
without touching any critical values of
W
; see Proposition
8.18
.

Given
\mathbf{Th}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}
, choose quadratic differentials
\phi^{\prime}
and cobordism data
(\mathfrak{b}_{A}^{\prime})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}}
to define the
A_{\infty}
-category

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\mathbf{Th}^{\prime};\mathcal{T}^{n+1},\phi^{\prime},(\mathfrak{b}_{A}^{\prime})).

The idea is to construct a quasi-isomorphism between
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th};\mathcal{T}^{n+1},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
which sends
\Lambda_{j}
to
\Lambda_{j}^{\prime}
. Note that the union
\widetilde{\mathbf{Th}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{Th}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}
is ordered such that

\Lambda_{0}\prec\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}\prec\Lambda_{1}\prec\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\prec\cdots\prec\Lambda_{n}\prec\Lambda_{n}^{\prime}.

Due to the inductive nature of Lemma
7.18
and Lemma
8.13
, for some
\tilde{\epsilon}>\epsilon
, one can find an
(\tilde{\epsilon},\mathcal{T}^{2n+2})
-consistent section
\tilde{\phi}
of the bundle
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2n+2}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{2n+2}
, a set of strip-like ends adapted to
\tilde{\phi}
, and consistent cobordism data
(\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{A})_{A\subset\widetilde{\mathbf{Th}}}
, extending the given ones on the subsets
\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{\mathbf{Th}}
. Then the
A_{\infty}
-category

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Th}};\mathcal{T}^{2n+2},\tilde{\phi},(\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{A})),

contains
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{E}
as
A_{\infty}
-subcategories. For every
0\leq j\leq n
, set the Hamiltonian perturbation
\delta H
in the Floer datum of
(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{j}^{\prime})
to be zero, so by Lemma
5.10
,

\hom_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{j}^{\prime})=\mathbb{F}\cdot e_{j}

is generated by the constant soliton
e_{j}
. Denote by
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]
the localization of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
at
\mathcal{T}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{e_{j}:0\leq j\leq n\}
(see Section
6.7
) and by
\pi:\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]
the quotient functor. Then we obtain a diagram:

(8.29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}

where
\eta,\eta^{\prime}
denote the inclusion functors. We shall not distinguish an object
Y\in\Ob\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
with its Yoneda image in the dg-category
\mathcal{Q}_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\rfmod(\tilde{\mathcal{E}})
of finite right
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
-modules. To compute the cohomological category of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]
, we exploit Lemma
6.13
. By Lemma
6.4
and
6.8
, for any
Y\in\Ob\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
, the triangle in the diagram below is exact:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\hom_{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\hom_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\hom_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}(\mathcal{C}one(e_{j}),Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\hom_{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}(\Lambda_{j},Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\hom_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}(\Lambda_{j},Y)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mu^{2}_{\mathcal{E}}](e_{j},\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The left horizontal arrows (both top and bottom) are given by the Yoneda embedding functor. If
Y\neq\Lambda_{j}
, then the left vertical map is an isomorphism by Proposition
8.18
, so the third group in the exact triangle is trivial

H(\hom_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}(\mathcal{C}one(e_{j}),Y))=0.

Hence, any
\Lambda_{k}^{\prime},0\leq k\leq n
is right orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}one(\mathcal{T})
. A similar argument shows that

\displaystyle H(\hom_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}(Y,\mathcal{C}one(e_{j})))

\displaystyle=H(\mathcal{C}one(e_{j})(Y))=0.

for all
Y\neq\Lambda_{j}^{\prime}
, which implies that any
\Lambda_{k}
is left orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}one(\mathcal{T})
. By Lemma
6.13
, we conclude that

H(\hom_{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}(X,Y))=H(\hom_{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]}(X,Y))

for all
X,Y\in\Ob\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
unless
(X,Y)=(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},\Lambda_{j}),0\leq j\leq n
. In the latter case, the group
H(\hom_{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]}(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},\Lambda_{j}))
is computed by Lemma
6.14
and contains an inverse of
[e_{j}]
. We conclude that

\pi\circ\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]\text{ and }\pi\circ\eta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]

are quasi-equivalences. A left inverse of
H(\pi\circ\eta^{\prime})

G:H(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}])\to H(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}),

is given by sending
\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{j}^{\prime}\mapsto\Lambda_{j}^{\prime}
, which is then lifted to a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{G}:\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\mathcal{T}^{-1}]\to\mathcal{E}
by Lemma
6.7
. Then the composition

(8.30)

l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\circ\pi\circ\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\ \Lambda_{j}\mapsto\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},\ 0\leq j\leq n

is the desired quasi-isomorphism. By Lemma
6.6
, this
A_{\infty}
-functor is canonical up to homotopy as the cohomological functor is independent of the choice of
\tilde{\phi}
and
(\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{A})_{A\subset\widetilde{\mathbf{Th}}}
by Lemma
8.20
. Indeed,
H(l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}})
is defined as a zigzag:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},\Lambda_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime},\Lambda_{k}^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{*}(e_{k},\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{*}(\cdot,e_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}})}

for all
j<k
. In particular, if
\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma
is admissible such that
\mathbf{Th}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}
, then
l_{\mathbf{Th}^{\prime},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}}\circ l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}}
is homotopic to
l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}}
. This shows that the
\mathcal{E}
is independent of the choice of
\phi
and
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}
: difference choices will yield directed
A_{\infty}
-categories quasi-isomorphic to the same
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
∎

The next corollary follows immediately from the proof of Proposition
8.21
.

Corollary 8.22
.

The directed
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
is independent of small variations of the angles
\theta_{j},0\leq j\leq n
up to canonical quasi-isomorphisms.

Remark 8.23
.

If one uses a general metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
for
\mathbf{Th}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
for
\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}
, it is not guaranteed that there is a metric ribbon tree
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
with
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}\cong\widetilde{\mathbf{Th}}
and which contains
\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
as subtrees. This is the main reason to take
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}^{n+1}
.
∎

8.10.
Invariance of Fukaya-Seidel categories

Given any admissible angle
\theta_{*}
, let
\Gamma_{\theta_{*}}
denote the space of admissible sets
\mathbf{Th}=(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{m}),\ \Lambda_{j}=\Lambda_{y_{j},\theta_{j}}
such that

(8.31)

\theta_{*}<\theta_{m}<\cdots<\theta_{1}<\theta_{*}^{\crit},

and
\Crit(W)=\{y_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
is ordered as in (
8.2
). Proposition
8.21
implies that the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}),\ \mathbf{Th}\in\Gamma_{\theta_{*}}
is independent of the choice of the quadratic differentials and the cobordism data. For this special case, there is a slightly different route to verify this invariance. Define a partial order on
\Gamma_{\theta_{*}}
as follows:

\mathbf{Th}=(\Lambda_{j})_{j=1}^{m}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}=(\Lambda_{j}^{\prime})_{j=1}^{m},\ \Lambda_{j}=\Lambda_{y_{j},\theta_{j}},\ \Lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\Lambda_{y_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime}},\ 1\leq j\leq m

if

\theta_{*}<\theta_{m}^{\prime}<\cdots<\theta_{1}^{\prime}<\theta_{m}<\cdots<\theta_{1}<\theta_{*}^{\crit}.

Proposition 8.24
.

For any pair
\mathbf{Th}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}
, there is a quasi-isomorphism

l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})\to\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}^{\prime})

such that
l_{\mathbf{Th}^{\prime},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}}\circ l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}}
is homotopic to
l_{\mathbf{Th},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}}
for any triple
\mathbf{Th}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same line of arguments as in Proposition
8.21
. The only difference arises from the fact that
\mathbf{Th}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}
is ordered such that

\Lambda_{1}\prec\Lambda_{2}\prec\cdots\prec\Lambda_{m}\prec\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\prec\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}\prec\cdots\prec\Lambda_{m}^{\prime}.

To verify that
\Lambda_{k}
is left orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}one(e_{j})
when
j<k
(the
j\geq k
case is identical to Proposition
8.21
) , one has to use the fact that

\HFF_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{k},\Lambda_{j}^{\prime})=0

for all
j<k
. This follows from Lemma
5.9
and the fact that
l_{y_{k},\theta_{k}}
does not intersect with
l_{y_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime}}
, since
\im(e^{-\theta_{*}}W(y_{j}))<\im(e^{-\theta_{*}}W(y_{k}))
and
\theta_{*}<\theta_{j}^{\prime}<\theta_{k}<\theta_{*}+\epsilon
; see Figure
26
below. The same property is used to verify that
\Lambda_{j}^{\prime}
is left orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}one(e_{k})
when
j<k
.
∎

\begin{overpic}[Pic41.png]
\put(-10.0,15.0){\small$H(y_{k})$}
\put(-10.0,2.0){\small$H(y_{j})$}
\put(42.0,20.0){\small$W(y_{k})$}
\put(35.0,7.0){\small$W(y_{j})$}
\put(90.0,40.0){\small$l_{y_{k},\theta_{k}}$}
\put(100.0,15.0){\small$l_{y_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime}}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 26
.
l_{y_{k},\theta_{k}}
does not interest with
l_{y_{j},\theta_{j}^{\prime}}
(
\theta_{*}=0
).

Remark 8.25
.

The partial order on
\Gamma
is inspired by the construction of wrapped Fukaya category
\mathcal{W}(M,W)
in
[
GPS20
, Section 3.5]
, and one should think of
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
as a subcategory of
\mathcal{W}(M,W)
if
(M,W)
is a Liouville Landau-Ginzburg model in the sense of
[
GPS20
, Example 2.20]
.
∎

8.11.
Invariance of the diagonal bimodule
\Delta

To construct the diagonal bimodule
\Delta
in Example
8.2
, one can be more flexible about the choice of the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
. This extra flexibility is essential to the proof of Theorem
8.3
, as we shall see shortly in Section
10
. Recall that
\theta_{*}=0
is assumed to be admissible in the sense of Example
8.1
, and let
\Gamma_{\Delta}
denote the space of triples
\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}=(R,\mathbf{Th}_{\pi},\mathbf{Th}_{0})
with
R\geq 0
,
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\in\Gamma_{\pi}
and
\mathbf{Th}_{0}\in\Gamma_{0}
. For any
\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}\in\Gamma_{\Delta}
, one can define a directed
A_{\infty}
-category

\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0};\mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{R},\phi,(\mathfrak{b}_{A}))

which may a priori depends on the choice of
R\geq 0
,
\phi
and the cobordism data
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})
.
\mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{R}
is the metric ribbon tree defined in Example
7.8
, which has two interior vertices and
2m
exterior ones. Any
S_{j}\in\mathbf{Th}_{0}
and
U_{k}\in\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}
have distance
=R+\pi
in
\mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{R}
. The subtree of
\mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{R}
associated to the subset
\mathbf{Th}_{0}
(resp.
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}
) is
\mathcal{T}^{m+1}
, so
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta})
contains
\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{0})
and
\mathcal{B}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi})
as
A_{\infty}
-subcategories. When
R=0
,
\mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{0}=\mathcal{T}^{2m}
, and
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta})
agrees with the previous construction.

Proposition 8.26
.

The
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta})
is independent of the choice of
\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}\in\Gamma_{\Delta}
,
\phi
and
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})
up to canonical quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

Define a partial order on
\Gamma_{\Delta}
such that

\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}=(R,\mathbf{Th}_{\pi},\mathbf{Th}_{0})\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}^{\prime}(R^{\prime},\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}^{\prime},\mathbf{Th}_{0}^{\prime})

if
R\leq R^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}_{\pi}
in
\Gamma_{\pi}
and
\mathbf{Th}_{0}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}_{0}^{\prime}
in
\Gamma_{0}
. Following the proof of Proposition
8.21
, one has to construct a directed
A_{\infty}
-category
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
with

\Ob\tilde{\mathcal{E}}=\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}^{\prime},

and which contains
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta})
and
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}^{\prime})
as
A_{\infty}
-subcategories. To this end, we use the metric ribbon tree from Example
7.9
: take
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}^{d_{1},d_{2},d_{3}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}
with

R_{1}=R,\ R_{2}=R^{\prime},\ d_{1}=2m\text{ and }d_{2}=d_{3}=m.

This means that
\mathcal{T}
has three interior vertices
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
with
v_{2}
lying on the interval
[v_{1},v_{3}]
and with

d_{\mathcal{T}}(v_{1},v_{2})=R\text{ and }d_{\mathcal{T}}(v_{1},v_{3})=R^{\prime}.

\begin{overpic}[Pic38.png]
\put(-5.0,2.0){$\mathfrak{o}_{0}$}
\put(13.0,20.0){$v_{1}$}
\put(51.0,20.0){$v_{2}$}
\put(90.0,20.0){$v_{3}$}
\put(32.0,19.0){$R$}
\put(65.0,19.0){$R^{\prime}-R$}
\put(3.0,-3.0){$\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}$}
\put(19.0,-3.0){$\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}^{\prime}$}
\put(50.0,-3.0){$\mathbf{Th}_{0}$}
\put(89.0,-3.0){$\mathbf{Th}_{0}^{\prime}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 27
.
The metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}^{6,3,3}_{R,R^{\prime}}
with
m=2
.

The first
2m
vertices in
\partial\mathcal{T}
are adjacent to
v_{1}
and labeled by
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}^{\prime}
. The next
m
vertices are adjacent to
v_{2}
and labeled by
\mathbf{Th}_{0}
, while the rest are adjacent to
v_{3}
and labeled by
\mathbf{Th}_{0}^{\prime}
. The rest of the proof is identical to that of Proposition
8.24
: using
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
one constructs a quasi-isomorphism

l_{\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}_{\Delta}}:\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta})\to\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}_{\Delta})

and verifies that
l_{\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}_{\Delta},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}_{\Delta}}\circ l_{\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime}_{\Delta}}
is homotopic to
l_{\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta},\mathbf{Th}^{\prime\prime}_{\Delta}}
for any triple
\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}
. Details are left to the readers.
∎

Remark 8.27
.

There are multiple ways to choose the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}
in the proof of Proposition
8.26
to define
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
. The advantage of using Example
7.9
is in that for any chain in
\Gamma_{\Delta}
:

\mathbf{Th}^{(1)}_{\Delta}\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{(2)}_{\Delta}\xrightarrow{s}\cdots\xrightarrow{s}\mathbf{Th}^{(n)}_{\Delta}

with
\mathbf{Th}^{(k)}_{\Delta}=(R_{k},\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}^{(k)},\mathbf{Th}_{0}^{(k)})
,
1\leq k\leq n
, one can use the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}_{R_{1},\cdots,R_{k}}^{d_{1},\cdots,d_{n+1}}
with

d_{1}=n\cdot m\text{ and }d_{k}=m=|\Crit(W)|,\ 2\leq k\leq n+1,

to construct a directed
A_{\infty}
-category that contains each
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}^{(k)})
as an
A_{\infty}
-subcategory, although this property is not used in the proof of Proposition
8.26
.
∎

8.12.
A generalization

There is one more preliminary step to go before we turn to the proof of Theorem
8.3
. Let
\mathbf{Th}
be any admissible set of thimbles and
\mathcal{T}
a metric ribbon tree with
n+1\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}|\partial\mathcal{T}|=|\mathbf{Th}|
. The combinatorial structure of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
as a smooth manifold with corners, as described by
n
-leafed trees, is critical to essentially any construction of Fukaya categories. On the other hand, the completions of
(n+1)
-pointed disks, which are used to define the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}
, are specified by a consistent section of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
, but this requirement can be relaxed a little bit: in general
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th})
can be defined using a smooth map
\phi^{\circ}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}
which is not necessarily a section.

In other words, we would like to distinguish
the parameter space
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}^{n+1}}
from
the actual family of pointed disks
(and their completions) on which the Floer equation (
5.5
) is defined. We explain this in more detail. From now on we always write
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}_{\base}
for this parameter space and
\phi^{\circ}
for a smooth map
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}_{\base}\to\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}
. For any subset
A\subset\mathbf{Th}
, the family
\mathcal{S}^{|A|}_{\base}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}
comes with a set of strip-like ends
\{\iota_{A,k}^{\base}\}_{k=0}^{|A|-1}
, not necessarily adapted to any quadratic differentials, and which defines for any
n
-leafed tree
T
a set of strip-like ends
\iota_{T}^{\base}=(\iota_{f}^{\base})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
on the family
\mathcal{S}^{T}_{\base}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}_{\base}
and for any pair
T<T^{\prime}
a gluing map

\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}_{\base}:(-1,0)^{\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}\times\mathcal{R}^{T}_{\base}\to\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}}_{\base}.

The set of strip-like ends
\{\iota_{T}^{\base}\}
is assumed to be consistent in the sense of
[
Sei08
, Lemma 9.3]
. In particular, the diagram
(
7.17
)
holds for any triple
T<T^{\prime}<T^{\prime\prime}
. The subscript (resp. superscript)
\base
is to indicate that these data are associated to the base manifold
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}_{\base}
, which are chosen once and for all. Now we spell out the conditions for the smooth map
\phi^{\circ}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}_{\base}\to\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}
.

•

for any subset
A\subset\mathbf{Th}
with
|A|\geq 3
, choose a smooth function
g_{A}^{\circ}:\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
and a smooth section
\psi^{\circ}_{A}
of
(g_{A}^{\circ})^{*}\mathcal{V}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}
that is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}_{A}
, i.e.,
\psi^{\circ}_{A}:\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}\to\mathcal{V}^{|A|}
is a smooth map covering
g_{A}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{|A|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g_{A}^{\circ})^{*}\mathcal{S}^{|A|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{|A|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{|A|},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{|A|}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{A}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\circ}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{A}^{\circ}}

•

choose a set of
\psi_{A}^{\circ}
-adapted strip-like ends
\{\iota^{\circ}_{A,k}\}_{k=0}^{|A|-1}
for the family
g_{A}^{*}\mathcal{S}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}
, that is, a set of proper embeddings,

\iota^{\circ}_{A,k}:\mathbb{R}^{\pm}_{t}\times[0,\mathfrak{w}_{A,k}^{\circ}]_{s}\times\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}\to(g_{A}^{\circ})^{*}\mathcal{S}^{|A|},

fibered over
\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}
, one for each
k
, which is
\psi_{A,r}^{\circ}
-adapted on the fiber
S_{g_{A}^{\circ}(r)}
for all
r\in\mathcal{R}^{|A|}_{\base}
. The width
\mathfrak{w}_{A,k}^{\circ}
of
\iota_{A,k}^{\circ}
is determined by the metric ribbon tree.

These data define for each
n
-leafed tree
T
,

•

a smooth map
g_{T}^{\circ}=(g_{T,v}^{\circ}):\mathcal{R}^{T}_{\base}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}
;

•

a smooth section
\phi_{T}^{\circ}=(\phi_{T,v}^{\circ})_{v\in\Ve(T)}
of
(g_{T}^{\circ})^{*}\mathcal{V}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}_{\base}
;

•

a set of
\phi_{T}^{\circ}
-adapted strip-like ends
\iota_{T}^{\circ}=(\iota_{f}^{\circ})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
for the family
g_{T}^{*}\mathcal{S}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T}_{\base}
;

which satisfy the following properties:

(i)

g_{T,v}^{\circ}=g_{A}^{\circ}
,
\phi_{T,v}^{\circ}=\psi_{A}^{\circ}
and
\iota_{f_{k}(v)}^{\circ}=\iota_{A,k}^{\circ},\ 0\leq k\leq|v|-1,
if
A=\partial\mathcal{T}_{v}
;

(ii)

g^{\circ}=(g_{T}^{\circ}):(\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}_{\base},\partial\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}_{\base})\to(\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1},\partial\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1})
is a smooth map of degree
1
;

(iii)

\phi^{\circ}=(\phi_{T}^{\circ})
is a smooth section of
(g^{\circ})^{*}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{d+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}_{\base}
;

(iv)

for any pair
T<T^{\prime}
,
\phi_{T^{\prime}}^{\circ}
is obtained by gluing
\phi_{T}^{\circ}
using the strip-like ends
(\iota^{\circ}_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
on the image of
\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}_{\base}
.

The last property

(

⁢
i
v

)

means that for any
T<T^{\prime}
there is a commutative diagram:

(8.32)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1,0)^{\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}\times\mathcal{R}^{T}_{\base}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1,0)^{\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}\times\mathcal{V}^{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}}_{\base}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{T^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}_{\base}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Id\times\phi_{T}^{\circ}}
glue
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{T^{\prime}}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{T^{\prime}}^{\circ}}
projection

The dashed arrow is not canonically defined. On the image of
\Id\times\phi^{\circ}_{T}
, it is the gluing map defined using the strip-like ends
(\iota^{\circ}_{f})_{f\in\Fl(T)}
and whose construction is outlined in Section
7.8
. The smooth map
g_{T^{\prime}}^{\circ}
is determined by
\phi_{T}^{\circ}
and the property

(

⁢
i
v

)

on
\im\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}_{\base}
.

Definition 8.28
.

We say that
\phi^{\circ}=(\phi^{\circ}_{T})
is an
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T})
-consistent family of quadratic differentials parametrized by
g^{\circ}=(g_{T}^{\circ}):\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\base}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
if
(\phi^{\circ},g^{\circ})
satisfies the conditions above for some choice of strip-like ends
(\iota^{\circ}_{T})
.
∎

Remark 8.29
.

When
|A|=3
,
g_{A}^{\circ}:\mathcal{R}_{\base}^{|A|}\to\mathcal{R}^{|A|}
is a bijection between points. In general,
g_{A}^{\circ}
can be extended smoothly into a map
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\base}^{|A|}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{|A|}
using (
8.32
), and one can verify inductively that this mapping degree is
1
; see Figure
28
. Thus the second property

(

⁢
i
i

)

is somewhat redundant.
∎

Example 8.30
.

If one simply takes
g^{\circ}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\base}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
to be the identity map in Definition
8.28
, and if the strip-like ends
\{\iota^{\base}_{T}\}
are already adapted to
\phi^{\circ}
, then Definition
8.28
recovers with the special case in Lemma
7.18
. For another possible reduction, one starts with an
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T})
-consistent section
\phi
of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
; then a set of
\phi
-adapted strip-like ends
(\iota_{T})
defines the gluing map

\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}:(-1,0)^{\Ed^{\inte}(T)\setminus\Ed^{\inte}(T^{\prime})}\times\mathcal{R}^{T}\to\mathcal{R}^{T^{\prime}}.

Take
g^{\circ}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\base}^{n+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{n+1}
to be any smooth diffeomorphism between manifolds with corners that intertwines
\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}_{\base}
and
\gamma^{T,T^{\prime}}
for any
T<T^{\prime}
, and define
\phi^{\circ}=(g^{\circ})^{*}\phi
. The upshot is that
g^{\circ}
is not required to be a diffeomorphism (or even injective) in Definition
8.28
.
∎

\begin{overpic}[Pic42.png]
\put(5.0,23.0){\small$\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{4}_{\base}\cong[0,1]$}
\put(110.0,20.0){\small$\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{4}\cong[0,1]$}
\put(110.0,50.0){\small the total space of}
\put(110.0,40.0){\small$\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{4}$}
\put(47.0,37.0){\small$\phi^{\circ}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 28
.
The red curve is the image of
\phi^{\circ}
where
n+1=4
.

Consider the smooth fiber bundle

(8.33)

\hat{S}^{|A|}_{\psi_{A}}\to\mathcal{R}_{\base}^{|A|}

whose fiber at
r\in\mathcal{R}_{\base}^{|A|}
is the completion of
(S_{g_{A}^{\circ}(r)},\psi^{\circ}_{A,r})
. The primary reason to introduce this framework is to bypass a technical difficulty that may arise otherwise in the proof of Theorem
8.3
; see Remark
9.12
. From now on we focus on the special case in Section
8.11
. For any triple
\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}=(R,\mathbf{Th}_{\pi},\mathbf{Th}_{0})\in\Gamma_{\Delta}
, one can choose some
(\phi^{\circ},g^{\circ})
which are
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T})
-consistent, along with consistent cobordism data
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}
, to construct a directed
A_{\infty}
-category

(8.34)

\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0};\ \mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{R},\phi^{\circ},(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}}),\ \mathbf{Th}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}.

The cobordism data
\mathfrak{b}_{A}=(K_{A},\Xi_{A},\delta\kappa_{A},\delta H_{A})
is defined similarly as in Definition
8.11
except that each term is now associated to the bundle (
8.33
). The moduli space (
8.19
) is then replaced by

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}^{2m}_{\base}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{2m-1};\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{Th}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\coprod_{r\in\mathcal{R}^{2m}_{\base}}\mathcal{M}_{S_{g^{\circ}(r)}}(\{p_{k}\}_{k=0}^{2m-1};\mathfrak{b}_{\psi^{\circ}(r)}).

The generalization of Proposition
8.26
then states the following.

Proposition 8.31
.

The directed
A_{\infty}
-category (
8.34
) is independent of the choice of
\mathbf{Th}_{\Delta}\in\Gamma_{\Delta}
,
(\phi^{\circ},g^{\circ})
and
(\mathfrak{b}_{A})
up to canonical quasi-isomorphisms.

Remark 8.32
.

The proof of Proposition
8.31
is identical to that of Proposition
8.26
and is omitted here. In the next section, we shall construct the smooth map
g^{\circ}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{2m}_{\base}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{2m+2}
and
\phi^{\circ}
explicitly, for which the consistency conditions are easily verified. The advantage of Proposition
8.31
is in that
g^{\circ}
need not be a diffeomorphism in order to define
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}
.
∎

9.
The Geometric Filtration

9.1.
Introduction

From now on fix some admissible sets
\mathbf{Th}_{0}\in\Gamma_{0}
and
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\in\Gamma_{\pi}
. Following the notations from Example
8.2
, we write

\displaystyle\mathbf{Th}_{0}

\displaystyle=(S_{1},\cdots,S_{m}),

\displaystyle S_{j}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{x_{j},\theta_{j}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}

\displaystyle=(U_{m},\cdots,U_{1}),

\displaystyle U_{j}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{x_{j},\eta_{j}},\ 1\leq j\leq m.

The critical set
\Crit(W)=\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
is ordered as in (
8.5
), and the angles
\theta_{j},\eta_{j},1\leq j\leq m
satisfy the relation (
8.4
). Take
0<\epsilon\ll 1
. Suppose that the
A_{\infty}
-categories

(9.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{0};\mathcal{T}^{m},\phi_{*},(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}_{0}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{Th}_{\pi};\mathcal{T}^{m},\phi_{*},(\mathfrak{b}_{A})_{A\subset\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}})

are already defined using a smooth
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T}^{m})
-consistent section
\phi_{*}
of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{m}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{m}
and some consistent cobordism data. As noted in Remark
8.4
, one may use some convenient quadratic differentials and cobordism data for the diagonal bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}
to verify Theorem
8.3
. The goal of this section is to describe a suitable setup so that
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}
is filtered by a sequence of sub-bimodules

(9.2)

0={}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}}\subset{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta^{(1)}_{\mathcal{B}}\subset\cdots\subset{}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta^{(m)}_{\mathcal{B}}={}_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{B}}.

This means that for each pair
(U_{j},S_{k})
, the complex
\Delta(U_{j},S_{k})
comes with a filtration

0=\Delta^{(0)}(U_{j},S_{k})\subset\Delta^{(1)}(U_{j},S_{k})\subset\cdots\subset\Delta^{(m)}(U_{j},S_{k})=\Delta(U_{j},S_{k})

which is preserved under the
A_{\infty}
-actions of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
. In this paper, we will not talk about the invariance of this filtered bimodules but only take it as a trick to prove Theorem
8.3
. The construction of
\Delta^{(l)},0\leq l\leq n
relies on the generalized framework Section
8.12
as well as a neck-stretching technique, so
\Delta^{(l)}=\Delta_{R}^{(l)}
depends on an extra stretching parameter
R\gg\pi
. This idea can be generalized further to the case of Remark
8.5
, but we shall focus the admissible set
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}
here to simplify our exposition.

9.2.
The energy filtration

We start with the filtered complex
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
and specify its Floer datum. Fix some
0<\delta\ll 1
. For any
1\leq j,k\leq m
, choose smooth monotone functions
\alpha_{j}^{un},\alpha_{k}^{st}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}^{un}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\pi&\text{if }s\geq\pi-\delta,\\
\eta_{j}-\pi&\text{if }s\leq\delta,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{st}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{k}&\text{if }s\geq\pi-\delta,\\
\pi&\text{if }s\leq\delta.\end{array}\right.

Then for any
R\geq\pi
, define
\alpha_{jk}^{R}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
by the formula

(9.7)

\alpha_{jk}^{R}(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llc}\theta_{k}&\text{ if }R+\pi\leq s,\\
\alpha_{k}^{st}(s-R)&\text{ if }R\leq s\leq R+\pi,\\
\pi&\text{ if }\pi\leq s\leq R,\\
\alpha_{j}^{un}(s)&\text{ if }0\leq s\leq\pi,\\
\eta_{j}-\pi&\text{ if }s\leq 0,\end{array}\right.

which is “a concatenation” of
\alpha_{j}^{un}
and
\alpha_{k}^{st}
. Let
\gamma_{jk}^{R}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}
denote the characteristic curve of
\alpha^{R}_{jk}(s)
which is normalized by the property that (see Figure
29
below)

•

\gamma_{jk}^{R}(s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\cdot e^{\eta_{j}-\pi}
for all
s<0
and
\in\mathbb{R}
for all
s\in[\pi,R-\pi]
.

If one takes
R_{0}\geq\pi
as a reference parameter, then for any
R\geq R_{0}
:

(9.8)

\gamma_{jk}^{R}(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\gamma_{jk}^{R_{0}}(s)&\text{ if }s\leq R_{0},\\
\gamma_{jk}^{R_{0}}(R_{0})+(s-R_{0})&\text{ if }R_{0}\leq s\leq R,\\
\gamma_{jk}^{R_{0}}(s)+(R-R_{0})&\text{ if }R\leq s.\end{array}\right.

\begin{overpic}[Pic43.png]
\put(-3.0,19.0){\small$\pi$}
\put(29.0,34.0){\small$\eta_{j}-\pi$}
\put(33.0,28.0){\small$\theta_{k}$}
\put(10.0,25.0){\small$\alpha_{k}^{st}(s)$}
\put(10.0,38.0){\small$\alpha_{j}^{un}(s)$}
\put(70.0,40.0){\small$e^{i(\eta_{j}-\pi)}\cdot\mathbb{R}^{+}$}
\put(42.0,0.0){\small$-e^{i\theta_{k}}$}
\put(95.0,32.0){\small$-ie^{i\beta_{*}}$}
\put(78.0,10.0){\small$\gamma_{jk}^{R}(s)$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 29
.
The function
\alpha_{jk}^{R}(s)
(left) and its characteristic curve
\gamma^{jk}_{R}(s)
(right).

Fix some
\frac{\pi}{2}<\beta_{*}<\frac{\pi}{2}+\min\{\theta_{m},\eta_{0}-\pi\}
. By the relation (
8.4
), there exists
\epsilon_{*}>0
such that for all
1\leq j,k\leq m
and
R\geq\pi
, we have

(9.9)

\re(e^{i(\beta_{*}-\alpha_{jk}^{R}(s))})>\epsilon_{*}.

Finally, we require that the perturbation 1-form
\delta H_{jk}^{R}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s},\mathcal{H})
be supported on
[0,\pi]_{s}\cup[R,R+\pi]_{s}
,
\delta H^{R}_{jk}\equiv 0
if
j=k
, and the estimate

(9.10)

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{s}}\|\delta H_{jk}^{R}\|_{L^{\infty}_{1}(M)}^{2}ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{s}}\|\delta H_{jk}^{R}\|_{L^{\infty}(M)}ds<\frac{1}{R},

holds for all
j,k
and
R\geq\pi.
; compare (
2.7
). If
\delta H_{jk}^{R}
is chosen generically, then the Floer datum
\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R}=(R+\pi,\alpha_{jk}^{R}(s),\beta_{*},\epsilon_{*},\delta H_{jk}^{R})
is admissible, and the Floer complex

\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R})

is well-defined. The key observation is that
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
carries a natural energy filtration when
R\gg\pi
. For any
x_{j}\in\Crit(W)
, choose a neighborhood
\mathcal{O}(x_{j})\subset M
of
x_{j}
such that
\mathcal{O}(x_{j})\cap\mathcal{O}(x_{k})=\emptyset
if
j\neq k
. The next two lemmas follow from the standard argument in Morse-Smale-Witten theory
[
KM07
, Section 2]
. In our case, they follow from (
9.10
), the energy estimate in Lemma
2.8
, the Local Compactness Hypothesis
3.1
and the fact that
W:M\to\mathbb{C}
is Morse.

Lemma 9.1
.

Take a sequence
R_{n}\to+\infty
, and let
p_{n}(s)\in\mathfrak{C}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R_{n}})
be any
\alpha_{jk}^{R_{n}}
-soliton. Then there exists a critical point
x_{l}\in\Crit(W)
, an
\alpha_{j}^{un}
-soliton
p^{un}_{j}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to M
connecting
x_{j}
with
x_{l}
, and an
\alpha_{k}^{st}
-soliton
p^{st}_{j}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to M
connecting
x_{l}
with
x_{k}
(and with zero perturbation 1-forms) such that a subsequence of
\{p_{n}\}
converges as broken flowlines to the concatenation of
p^{un}_{j}
and
p^{st}_{k}
. This means that for this subsequence

p_{n}(s)\to p_{j}^{un}(s)\text{ and }p_{n}(s-R_{n})\to p_{k}^{st}(s)\text{ in }C^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}_{s},M).

Lemma 9.2
.

Under above assumptions, there exist constants
C,\zeta>0
such that for any
1\leq j,k\leq m
,
R\geq\pi
and any
\alpha_{jk}^{R}
-soliton
p(s)\in\mathfrak{C}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R})
, we have estimates

(9.11)

\displaystyle|\partial_{s}p(s)|^{2}+|\nabla H\circ p(s)|^{2}

\displaystyle<Ce^{-\zeta\min\{|s|,|s-(R+\pi)|\}},\ \forall s\in\mathbb{R}_{s}

(9.12)

\displaystyle|W(p(s))-W(x_{j})|

\displaystyle<Ce^{-\zeta|s|},\ \forall s\leq 0,

(9.13)

\displaystyle|W(p(s))-W(x_{k})|

\displaystyle<Ce^{-\zeta|s-(R+\pi)|},\ \forall s\geq R+\pi,

and for some
x_{l}\in\Crit(W)
, there holds

(9.14)

|W(p(s))-W(x_{l})|<Ce^{-\zeta\min\{|s|,|s-(R+\pi)|\}},\ \forall s\in[\pi,R]_{s}.

In particular, by (
9.11
), there exists a constant
R_{0}>0
such that for any
R\geq 2R_{0}
and
R_{0}\leq s\leq R-R_{0}
,
p(s)\in\mathcal{O}(x_{l})
.

\begin{overpic}[Pic44.png]
\put(100.0,62.0){\small$S_{1}$}
\put(-5.0,0.0){\small$U_{4}$}
\put(46.0,53.0){\small$H(q_{4})$}
\put(46.0,40.0){\small$H(q_{3})$}
\put(46.0,28.0){\small$H(q_{2})$}
\put(40.0,16.0){\small$H(q_{1})$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 30
.
The energy filtration on
\Delta(U_{4},S_{1})
.

For any
R\geq 2R_{0}
, Lemma
9.2
allows us to decompose the space of solitons as

\mathfrak{C}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R})=\coprod_{l=0}^{m}\mathfrak{C}^{l}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R})

such that
p(s)\in\mathfrak{C}^{l}
only if the
\alpha_{jk}^{R}
-soliton
p(s)
approximates
x_{l}\in\Crit(W)
on the interval
[R_{0},R-R_{0}]_{s}
. Let

\mathcal{G}^{l}\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})=\bigoplus_{p\in\mathfrak{C}^{l}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R})}\mathbb{K}\cdot p,

denote the subspace of
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
freely generated by solitons in
\mathfrak{C}^{l}
. Then

\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})=\bigoplus_{l=0}^{m}\mathcal{G}^{l}\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k}).

Lemma 9.3
.

For all
R\gg\pi
,
\mathcal{G}^{l}\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
is trivial unless
k\leq l\leq j
.

Proof of Lemma
9.3
.

It suffices to note that for the limiting solitons
p^{un}_{j}
and
p^{st}_{k}
in Lemma
9.1
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}H(p^{un}_{j}(s))

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla H,\partial_{s}p^{un}_{j}(s)\rangle=\langle\nabla H,-\nabla\re(e^{-i\alpha_{j}^{un}(s)}W)\rangle\leq 0,

\displaystyle\partial_{s}H(p^{st}_{k}(s))

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla H,\partial_{s}p^{st}_{k}(s)\rangle=\langle\nabla H,-\nabla\re(e^{-i\alpha_{k}^{st}(s)}W)\rangle\leq 0,

because
\alpha_{j}^{un}(s)\in[\eta_{j}-\pi,\pi]
and
\alpha_{k}^{st}(s)=[\theta_{j},\pi]
. This implies that

H(x_{j})\geq H(x_{l})\geq H(x_{k}),

or equivalently
k\leq l\leq j
.
∎

Lemma
9.3
has an immediate corollary.

Corollary 9.4
.

For all
R\gg\pi
,
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
is trivial if
j<k
. Since
\delta H_{jk}^{R}\equiv 0
when
j=k
,
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{j})=\mathcal{G}^{j}\Delta(U_{j},S_{j})\cong\mathbb{K}\cdot e_{x_{j}}
is generated the quasi-unit
e_{x_{j}}
, i.e., the constant soliton at
x_{j}\in\Crit(W)
; cf. Proposition
8.18
.

Lemma 9.5
.

There exists a constant
C
independent of
R
or the perturbation 1-form
\delta H_{jk}^{R}
such that for all
1\leq j,k\leq m
,
R\geq 2R_{0}
and
p(s)\in\mathfrak{C}^{l}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R})
, we have

(9.15)

|\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R}}(p)+R\cdot H(x_{l})|<C.

Proof.

The formula of
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R}}(p)
is given by (
2.2
); then (
9.15
) can be proved as follows. By Lemma
9.2
, the function

\im(e^{-i\alpha_{jk}^{R}(s)}W(p(s)))=-H(p(s)),\ s\in[\pi,R]_{s}

is “almost constant” on this interval. In particular, (
9.14
) implies that the difference

|(R-\pi)\cdot H(x_{l})-\int_{\pi}^{R}H(p(s))ds|<C_{1}

is uniformly bounded by some constant
C_{1}>0
. Similarly, the differences

\int_{-\infty}^{0}|\im(e^{-i\eta_{j}}(W(p(s))-W(x_{j})))|\text{ and }\int_{R+\pi}^{\infty}|\im(e^{-i\theta_{k}}(W(p(s))-W(x_{k})))|.

are controlled by (
9.12
) and (
9.13
). Finally, the integral
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{s}}p^{*}\lambda_{M}
is also bounded for all
R\gg\pi
. Indeed, if
p_{n}(s)\in\mathfrak{C}^{l}(U_{j},S_{k};\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R_{n}})
is a converging subsequence in Lemma
9.1
as
R_{n}\to\infty
, then

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{s}}p_{n}^{*}\lambda_{M}\to\int_{\mathbb{R}_{s}}(p_{j}^{un})^{*}\lambda_{M}+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{s}}(p_{k}^{st})^{*}\lambda_{M},

as
n\to\infty
. This follows from the convergence in
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-topology in Lemma
9.1
and the decay estimate in Lemma
9.2
. In fact, one can show that

\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R_{n}}}(p_{n})+(R_{n}-\pi)\cdot H(x_{l})\to\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{j}^{un}}(p_{j}^{un})+\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{k}^{st}}(p_{k}^{st})

as
n\to\infty
, where
\mathfrak{a}_{j}^{un}=(\pi,\alpha_{j}^{un}(s),\beta,\epsilon_{*},\delta H\equiv 0)
and
\mathfrak{a}_{k}^{st}=(\pi,\alpha_{k}^{st}(s),\beta,\epsilon_{*},\delta H\equiv 0)
. This proves (
9.15
).
∎

Note that the moduli space
\widecheck{\mathcal{M}}(p_{-},p_{+})
in (
2.14
) is alway empty if

\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{jk}}(p_{-})<\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{jk}}(p_{+}),

so the Floer differential on
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
can only increase the value of
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{jk}^{R}}
. Lemma
9.5
then implies that for all
R\gg\pi
and
p_{\pm}\in\mathfrak{C}^{l_{\pm}}
,
\widecheck{\mathcal{M}}(p_{-},p_{+})
is empty if
l_{-}>l_{+}
. Hence

(9.16)

\Delta^{(l)}_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigoplus_{n=1}^{l}\mathcal{G}^{n}\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k}),0\leq n\leq m

is a subcomplex of
\Delta(U_{j},S_{k})
, and

\mathcal{G}^{l}\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})=\Delta^{(l)}_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})/\Delta^{(l-1)}_{R}(U_{j},S_{k}),1\leq l\leq m

is the associated graded complex. By Lemma
5.9
, for
j\neq k
,
H(\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k}))
is independent of
R\geq\pi
and is always trivial. However, since the Floer data are chosen differently here, the complex
\Delta_{R}(U_{j},S_{k})
might be pretty large when
j>k
; see Figure
30
.

9.3.
The filtered bimodule

Having specified the Floer data for any pairs of objects in
\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}
, the next step is to define the
A_{\infty}
-operations

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{R}}^{r|1|s}:\hom_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{k_{r-1}},S_{k_{r}})\otimes\cdots

\displaystyle\otimes\hom_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{k_{0}},S_{k_{1}})\otimes\Delta_{R}(U_{j_{s}},S_{k_{0}})

\displaystyle\otimes\hom_{\mathcal{B}}(U_{j_{s-1}},U_{j})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hom_{\mathcal{B}}(U_{j_{0}},U_{j_{1}})\to\Delta_{R}(U_{j_{0}},S_{k_{r}})

for all
r+s\geq 1
such that

(9.17)

\mu_{\Delta_{R}}^{r|1|s}(a_{r},\cdots,a_{1},x,b_{s},\cdots,b_{1})\in\Delta_{R}^{(l)}(U_{j_{0}},S_{k_{r}}).

if
x\in\Delta^{(l)}_{R}(U_{j_{s}},S_{k_{0}})
. This proves that each
\Delta^{(l)}_{R}
is a sub-bimodule of
\Delta_{R}
. As usual we carry out the construction for the extended
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{E}_{R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}_{\Delta_{R}}
with
\Ob\mathcal{E}_{R}=\mathbf{Th}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{Th}_{\pi}\sqcup\mathbf{Th}_{0}
. (
9.17
) will then follow from an energy inequality, but this is only possible if the quadratic differentials and cobordism data for
\mathcal{E}_{R}
are chosen carefully. We shall use the metric ribbon tree
\mathcal{T}^{m,m}_{R}
to define
\mathcal{E}_{R}
.

For any
R\geq 4\pi
, denote by
Z_{R}=\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[2\pi,R-2\pi]_{s}
the infinite strip of width
R-4\pi
equipped with the product metric. Let
(S,\phi_{R})
be any
(d+1)
-pointed disk equipped with an
S
-compatible quadratic differential that is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}_{R}^{d_{1},d_{2}}
with
d_{1}+d_{2}=d+1
and
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 1
, and let
\{\iota_{k}\}_{k=0}^{d}
be a set of strip-like ends adapted to
\phi_{R}
. For
0<\epsilon\ll 1
, there is an isometric embedding (see Figure
18
)

\tau:Z_{R}=\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[2\pi,R-2\pi]_{s}\to S

such that

(9.18)

\tau(t,s)=\iota_{0}(t,s),\ \forall t\leq 0

and for some
\xi_{\phi}>0
and
t_{\phi}>0

(9.19)

\tau(t+t_{\phi},s)=\iota_{d_{1}}(t,s+\xi_{\phi}),\ \forall t\geq 0.

The embedding
\tau
is uniquely determined by the first property (
9.18
). Denote by
\mathcal{T}_{\phi}
the metric ribbon tree induced by
\phi
, and write
\partial\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\cong\partial\mathcal{T}_{R}^{d_{1},d_{2}}=(\mathfrak{o}_{0},\cdots,\mathfrak{o}_{d})
. Then the constant
\xi_{\phi}
in (
9.19
) determined by the relation

\xi_{\phi}=d_{\mathcal{T}_{R}^{d_{1},d_{2}}}(\mathfrak{o}_{0},\mathfrak{o}_{d_{1}})-d_{\mathcal{T}_{\phi}}(\mathfrak{o}_{0},\mathfrak{o}_{d_{1}});

see Figure
31
below. Since
\mathcal{T}_{\phi}
is
\epsilon
-close to
\mathcal{T}_{R}^{d_{1},d_{2}}
in the sense of Definition of
7.10
,
|\xi_{\phi}|\leq\epsilon\ll 1
.

Remark 9.6
.

The author did not figure out a short argument to show that there is a
(\epsilon,\mathcal{T}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{R})
-consistent section
\phi
of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{d+1}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{d+1}
such that
\xi_{\phi_{r}}\equiv 0
for all
r\in\mathcal{R}^{d+1}
. However, this does not complexify our construction significantly. Readers may pretend that
\xi_{\phi}\equiv 0
when first reading this proof.
∎
