11.2.
Proof of Lemma
11.2
: some estimates about solitons

As usual, these gluing theorems are proved using the implicit function theorem
[
AD14
, Lemma 9.4.4]
or
[
MS12
, Propsoiton A.3.4]
, and the key ingredient is to estimate the right inverse of the linearized operator at a pre-gluing configuration. Although Lemma
11.2
is standard and follows immediately from the argument of
[
AD14
, Section 14]
, we summarize some key estimates as a warmup for the more sophisticated case of Theorem
11.3
. The upshot is that all such estimates must be uniform in the stretching parameter
R
. From now on we assume that
k=1,j=m
to simplify our notations; the general case will follow by the same argument. The subscripts
j,k
are saved later for different purposes. For simplicity, write

\mathfrak{C}^{st}=\mathfrak{C}(S_{\star},S_{1};\mathfrak{a}^{st}_{1}),\ \mathfrak{C}^{un}=\mathfrak{C}(U_{m},U_{\star};\mathfrak{a}^{un}_{m})\text{ and }\mathfrak{C}^{R}=\mathfrak{C}^{l}(U_{m},S_{1};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}).

To start, choose a cutoff function
\chi:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to[0,1]
such that
\chi(s)\equiv 0
if
s\leq 0
and
\equiv 1
if
s\geq 1
, and define

\displaystyle\chi^{un}_{R}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\chi\big(3-\frac{8s}{R+\pi}\big),

\displaystyle\chi^{st}_{R}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\chi\big(\frac{8s}{R+\pi}-5\big).

Then
(\chi^{un}_{R},\chi^{st}_{R})(s)\equiv(1,0)
if
s\leq\frac{R+\pi}{4}
and
\equiv(0,1)
if
s\geq\frac{3(R+\pi)}{4}
and
\equiv(0,0)
for
s\in[\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8},\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s}
. For any pair of thimbles
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1}),\ \Lambda_{n}=\Lambda_{q_{n},\theta_{n}}
,
n=1,2
, let
\mathcal{P}_{r}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}),r\geq 2
denote the space of smooths paths
\mathbb{R}_{s}\to M
which connect
q_{0},q_{1}
and have finite
L^{r}_{1}
-distance to a model path; in contrast to (
2.9
), we use the
L^{r}_{1}
-norm instead of
L^{2}_{k}
. Let
R_{0}>0
. If
p^{un}\in\mathcal{P}_{r}(U_{j},U_{\star})
and
p^{st}\in\mathcal{P}_{r}(S_{\star},S_{k})
satisfy that
p^{un}(s),p^{st}(s)\in\mathcal{O}(x_{l})
for all
s\geq R_{0}
, where
\mathcal{O}(x_{l})
is a normal neighborhood of
x_{l}
, then for
R\geq 4R_{0}
, there is a pre-gluing map

(11.7)

\Phi_{R}:\mathcal{P}_{r}(S_{\star},S_{1})\times\mathcal{P}_{r}(U_{m},U_{\star})\dashrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{r}(U_{m},S_{1})

such that
(p^{st},p^{un})
is sent to the path

\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})(s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}p^{st}(s-R)&\text{ if }s\geq\frac{3(\pi+R)}{4},\\
\exp_{x_{l}}\big(\chi^{un}_{R}(s)\cdot\exp_{x_{l}}^{-1}(p^{un}(s))+\chi^{st}_{R}(s)\cdot\exp_{x_{l}}^{-1}(p^{st}(s))\big)&\text{ otherwise, }\\
p^{un}(s)&\text{ if }s\leq\frac{\pi+R}{4}.\end{array}\right.

By construction,
\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})(s)\equiv x_{l}
for
s\in[\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8},\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s}
. If
p^{st},p^{un}
are
\alpha^{st}_{1}
-and
\alpha^{un}_{m}
-solitons respectively, then
p^{st}(-s),p^{un}(s)\to x_{l}
exponentially as
|s|\to\infty
. We use
p^{\pre}_{R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})
as the pre-gluing configuration and construct an actual
\alpha_{m1}^{R}
-soliton which is close to
p^{\pre}_{R}
using Newton-Picard iteration when
R\gg\pi
. The key ingredient in this argument is the following estimate.

Lemma 11.5
.

For any
r\geq 2
, there exists a constant
C_{1}=C_{1}(r)>0
with the following property. For any
R\gg\pi
,
p^{st}\in\mathfrak{C}^{st}
and
p^{un}\in\mathfrak{C}^{un}
, the operator

\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p^{\pre}_{R}):L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{R})^{*}TM)\to L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{R})^{*}TM)

is invertible for
p^{\pre}_{R}=\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})
. Moreover, its inverse is bounded by
C_{1}
in the operator norm.

Sketch of Proof.

Consider the following truncations of
p^{st},p^{un}
:

\displaystyle p^{un}_{R}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})(s)&\text{ if }s\leq\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8},\\
x_{l}&\text{ otherwise, }\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle p^{st}_{R}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})(s+R)&\text{ if }s+R\geq\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8},\\
x_{l}&\text{ otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

For any
R\gg\pi
, one can think of
p^{un}_{R}(s)
as a section of
L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{un})^{*}TM)
using the exponential map along
p^{un}
, and
p^{un}_{R}\to p^{un}
as
R\to\infty
in
L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{un})^{*}TM)
for all
r\geq 2
. The same holds also for
p^{st}_{R}
. This shows that
\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{st}}(p^{st}_{R})
and
\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{m}^{un}}(p^{un}_{R})
are invertible with uniformly bounded inverses.

\begin{overpic}[Pic58.png]
\put(3.0,2.0){$R_{*}$}
\put(25.0,2.0){$\frac{R_{*}}{4}$}
\put(36.0,2.0){$\frac{3R_{*}}{8}$}
\put(58.0,2.0){$\frac{5R_{*}}{8}$} \put(70.0,2.0){$\frac{3R_{*}}{4}$}
\put(90.0,2.0){$R_{*}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R+\pi$} \put(7.0,15.0){$\chi_{R}^{un}$} \put(90.0,15.0){$\chi_{R}^{st}$} \put(35.0,15.0){$\kappa_{R}^{un}$} \put(60.0,15.0){$\kappa_{R}^{st}$} \end{overpic}

Figure 37
.
The cutoff functions.

The pre-gluing configuration
p^{\pre}_{R}=\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})
is a “concatenation” of
p^{un}_{R}
and
p^{st}_{R}
. To deduce the invertibility of
\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}
at
p^{\pre}_{R}=\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})
, consider the cutoff functions

\kappa^{un}(s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\cos\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2}\cdot\chi(s)\bigg)\text{ and }\kappa^{st}(s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2}\cdot\chi(s)\bigg).

Then
(\kappa^{un})^{2}+(\kappa^{st})^{2}=1
,
(\kappa^{un},\kappa^{st})(s)=(1,0)
if
s\leq 0
and
=(0,1)
if
s\geq 1
.

\kappa^{un}_{R}(s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\kappa^{un}(\frac{4s}{R+\pi}-\frac{3}{2})\text{ and }\kappa^{st}_{R}(s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\kappa^{st}(\frac{4s}{R+\pi}-\frac{3}{2}).

Then
(\kappa^{un}_{R},\kappa^{st}_{R})(s)\equiv(1,0)
when
s\leq\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8}
and
\equiv(0,1)
when
s\geq\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}
. Now consider the transformation

\displaystyle U_{R}:L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{st}_{R})^{*}TM)\oplus L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{un}_{R})^{*}TM)

\displaystyle\to L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};T_{x_{l}}M)\oplus L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{R})^{*}TM)

(11.8)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}v^{st}\\
v^{un}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\kappa^{un}_{R}&-\kappa^{st}_{R}\\
\kappa^{st}_{R}&\kappa^{un}_{R}\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}v^{st}(s-R)\\
v^{un}(s)\end{pmatrix}

with the inverse
U_{R}^{-1}
defined by a similar matrix operator. Let

D_{x_{l}}=J\partial_{s}-\Hess_{x_{l}}:L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};T_{x_{l}}M)\to L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};T_{x_{l}}M),\ r\geq 2

denote the self-adjoint operator associated to the constant path at
x_{l}
. Then on the interval
[\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8},\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s}
, the operators

D_{x_{l}}=\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{m}^{un}}(p^{un}_{R})=\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un}))

are the same as
\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{st}}(p^{st}_{R})
on
[\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8}-R,\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}-R]_{s}
. With this in mind, one verifies that

\displaystyle U^{-1}_{R}\circ\big(D_{x_{l}}\oplus\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un}))\big)

\displaystyle\circ U_{R}

(11.9)

\displaystyle=\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{st}}(p^{st}_{R})\oplus\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{m}^{un}}(p^{un}_{R})+\delta_{R}

where the error term
\delta_{R}:L^{r}_{1}\to L^{r}_{1}
involves only the derivatives of
\kappa_{R}^{un},\kappa_{R}^{st}
and has norm bounded by
C/R
for some constant
C>0
. Since the operator norms of
U_{R},U_{R}^{-1}
are uniformly bounded, this proves that
\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un}))
is invertible, and its inverse is bounded in the operator norm by some uniform
C_{1}>0
in dependent of
R\gg\pi
.
∎

Using
[
AD14
, Lemma 9.4.4]
we can now construct a genuine soliton
p^{st}\circ_{R}p^{un}\in\mathfrak{C}^{R}
which is
L^{r}_{1}
-close to
p^{\pre}_{R}=\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un})
for
R\gg\pi
. To conclude the proof of Lemma
11.2
, one has to verify an additional compactness property: suppose that
p_{n}\in\mathfrak{C}^{R_{n}}
is any sequence of solitons with
R_{n}\to\infty
and
p_{n}\to(p^{st},p^{un})
in the sense of Lemma
9.1
, then for any
n\gg 1
, then
p_{n}
is
C^{0}
-close to
p^{\pre}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Phi_{R_{n}}(p^{st},p^{un})
and so can be viewed as a section of
(p^{\pre}_{n})^{*}TM
using the exponential map along. With this understood, we have

(11.10)

\|p_{n}-p_{n}^{\pre}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{n})^{*}TM)}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
for all
r\geq 2
. This is a stronger statement than the
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-convergence in Lemma
9.1
and implies that
p_{n}=p^{st}\circ_{R_{n}}p^{un}
for all
n\gg 1
in this sequence, by the uniqueness part of
[
AD14
, Lemma 9.4.4]
. In order to verify that
\gr(p^{st}\circ_{R}p^{un})=\gr(p^{un})+\gr(p^{st})
, note that replacing
p^{st}
by
p^{st}_{R}
,
p^{un}
by
p^{un}_{R}
and
p^{st}\circ_{R}p^{un}
by
p^{\pre}_{R}
does not introduce any spectral flow to the Hessians for all
R\gg\pi
. One can perturb these paths further to reduce this grading computation to the operators with Lagrangian boundary conditions using the Axiom
(A’-II)
. Finally, by Axiom
(A-II)
, it suffices to verify that if
\Pi^{un},\Pi^{st}
are any graded linear Lagrangian subspaces of
T_{x_{l}}M
such that
\Pi^{un}\pitchfork\Pi^{st}
,
\Pi^{un}\pitchfork T_{x_{l}}U_{\star}
and
T_{x_{l}}S_{\star}\pitchfork\Pi^{st}
, then

(11.11)

i(T_{x_{l}}S_{\star},\Pi^{st})+i(\Pi^{un},T_{x_{l}}U_{\star})=i(\Pi^{un},\Pi^{st}).

Note that
i(T_{x_{l}}S_{\star},T_{x_{l}}U_{\star})=i(T_{x_{l}}\Lambda_{x_{l},2\pi},T_{x_{l}}\Lambda_{x_{l},\pi})=0
by our grading convention. Then (
11.11
) follows from the general fact that

i(\Pi_{0}^{\#},\Pi_{1}^{\#})+i(\Pi_{2}^{\#},\Pi_{3}^{\#})=i(\Pi_{0}^{\#},\Pi_{3}^{\#})+i(\Pi_{2}^{\#},\Pi_{1}^{\#})

for any graded linear Lagrangian subspaces
\Pi_{j}^{\#},0\leq j\leq 3
of
T_{x_{l}}M
whenever these indices are defined; this follows from the defining property of the Maslov index; see
[
Sei08
, Section (11h)]
. This completes the proof of Lemma
11.2
.

11.3.
Proof of Theorem
11.3
Part I: Some estimates about instantons

From now on fix some solitons

p^{st}_{\pm}\in\mathfrak{C}^{st},p^{un}_{\pm}\in\mathfrak{C}^{un},

and let

p^{\pre}_{R,\pm}=\Phi_{R}(p^{st}_{\pm},p^{un}_{\pm}),\ p_{R,\pm}=p^{st}_{\pm}\circ_{R}p^{un}_{\pm}\in\mathfrak{C}^{R}.

p_{R,\pm}
are non-degenerate critical points of
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}
(solitons), while
p^{\pre}_{R,\pm}
are the pre-gluing configuration approximating
p_{R,\pm}
. By Lemma
11.5
and (
11.10
),

(11.12)

\|\Hess^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p_{R,\pm})\|_{L^{r}\to L^{r}_{1}}<C_{1}^{\prime}

for some constant
C_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}(r)
. Then by Lemma
9.5
, the drop of the action functional
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}

0\leq\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R}}(p_{R,-})-\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R}}(p_{R,+})<C_{2}

is bounded by a uniform constant
C_{2}>0
. This property is special to the associated graded complex
\mathcal{G}^{l}\Delta(U_{m},S_{1})
. Combined with Lemma
2.8
, this implies that for some
C_{3}>0
, we have

(11.13)

\mathcal{E}_{an}(P_{R};I_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})<C_{3},\ I_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[t-1,t+1]_{t}

for any
\alpha_{m1}^{R}
-instanton
P_{R}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{R}^{-},p_{R}^{+};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1})
and
t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}
. The next lemma allows us to control the solution
P_{R}
over the strip
Z_{R}=\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[2\pi,R-\pi]_{s}
on which the
\alpha_{m1}^{R}
-instanton equation (
2.12
) takes the standard form

\partial_{t}P_{R}-J\partial_{s}P_{R}-\nabla H=0.

Lemma 11.6
.

There exists constants
C_{4},R_{4},\zeta>0
with the following property. For any
R+\pi>2R_{4}
and any
\alpha_{m1}^{R}
-instanton
P_{R}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{R,-},p_{R,+};\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R})
, we have
P_{R}(t,s)\in\mathcal{O}(x_{l})
and

0\leq u_{R}(t,s)\leq C_{4}\cdot\frac{\cosh(\zeta\cdot(\frac{R+\pi}{2}-s))}{\cosh(\zeta\cdot\frac{R+\pi}{2})}

if
s\in[R_{4},(R+\pi)-R_{4}]_{s}
, where
u_{R}
is the energy density function of
P_{R}
.

Proof of Lemma
11.6
.

The proof follows the same line of arguments as in Proposition
3.6
. To start, we have to show a uniform energy decay as in Lemma
3.7
, but this is easy: the estimate (
11.13
) combined with our assumption that the angle
\theta=0
is admissible implies the analogue of Lemma
3.8
, then we deduce the uniform energy decay using the triviality of point-like solutions, i.e., Lemma
3.9
. To verify this exponential decay, we exploit the inequality

0\geq(\Delta+\zeta^{2})|dP_{R}|^{2}

as in the proof of Proposition
3.6
but now on the strip
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[R_{4},(R+\pi)-R_{4}]_{s}
for some
R_{4}\gg\pi
. Then we use the maximal principle from
[
Wan20a
, Corollary A.4]
to conclude.
∎

The next step is to control the behavior of
P
when the drop of the action functional
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}
is small on some
I_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}
,
I_{t}=[t-1,t+1]_{t}
; cf.
[
KM07
, Proposition 13.4.7]
. Chose
r_{0}>0
such that
r_{0}
is less then the injective radius of
(M,g_{M})
and

(11.14)

r_{0}<\frac{1}{2}\cdot\min\{\dist_{C^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}(p^{st}_{0},p^{st}_{1}),\dist_{C^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}(p^{un}_{0},p^{un}_{1}):p^{st}_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}^{st},\ p^{un}_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}^{un},\ j=0,1\}.

Lemma 11.7
.

Let
I_{t}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[t-\frac{1}{2},t+\frac{1}{2}]_{t}
. For any
r\geq 2
, there exist constants
\epsilon_{5},C_{5}
and
C_{6}=C_{6}(r)>0
with the following properties. For any solitons
p_{R,\pm}\in\mathfrak{C}^{R}
, any instanton
P_{R}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{R,-},p_{R,+};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1})
and
t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}
, suppose that

(11.15)

\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(P_{R}(t-1,\cdot))-\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(P_{R}(t+1,\cdot))<\epsilon_{5},

then for some soliton
p_{R}\in\mathfrak{C}^{R}
,
P_{R}(t^{\prime},\cdot)
has
C^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{s})
-distance
<r_{0}
with
p_{R}
for all
t^{\prime}\in I_{t}^{\prime}
. This
p_{R}
is unique due to our choice (
11.14
) of
r_{0}
. Then
P_{R}|_{I_{t}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}}
can be viewed as a section of
(p_{R})^{*}TM
using the exponential map along
p_{R}
, and we have the estimate

(11.16)

\|P_{R}|_{I_{t}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}}-p_{R}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(I_{t}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})}\leq C_{6}(r)\bigg(\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}\big(P_{R}(t-1,\cdot)\big)-\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}\big(P_{R}(t+1,\cdot)\big)\bigg),

where
p_{R}
is viewed as a constant trajectory on
I_{t}^{\prime}
. Moreover, for all
t^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}_{t}
,

(11.17)

\big|\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}\big(P_{R}(t^{\prime},\cdot))-\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p_{R})\big|\leq C_{5}\|\grad\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}\big(P_{R}(t^{\prime},\cdot)\big)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}.

Proof.

This lemma follows from the standard analysis of Floer trajectories; see for instance
[
KM07
, Section 13.4]
. The reason why
\epsilon_{5},C_{5},C_{6}
can be made uniform in
R
is (
11.12
): the Hessians of
\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}
can be inverted uniformly at all solitons in
\mathfrak{C}^{R}
for
R\gg\pi
.
∎

11.4.
Proof of Theorem
11.4
Part II: Compactness of instantons

With Lemma
11.6
and Lemma
11.7
at hands, we are ready to analyze the limit of
P_{R}
as
R\to\infty
. Recall that the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(p_{R,-},p_{R,+};\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R})
carries an
\mathbb{R}_{t}
-action: let
\tau_{t}P=P(\cdot-t,\cdot),t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}
.

Lemma 11.8
.

For any sequence of
\alpha^{R_{n}}_{m1}
-instantons
P_{n}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{R_{n},-},p_{R_{n},+};\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R_{n}})
with
R_{n}\to\infty
, there exists a subsequence converging to a broken instanton:

(11.18)

(p^{st}_{0},p^{un}_{0})\xrightarrow{(P^{st}_{0},P^{un}_{0})}(p^{st}_{1},p^{st}_{1})\to\cdots\to(p^{st}_{k},p^{un}_{k})\xrightarrow{(P^{st}_{k_{1}},P^{un}_{k_{1}})}(p^{st}_{k_{1}+1},p^{un}_{k_{1}+1}),

in the following sense:

•

for every
0\leq k\leq k_{1}
,
p^{st}_{k}\in\mathfrak{C}^{st}
and
p^{un}_{k}\in\mathfrak{C}^{un}
are some solitons;

•

(p^{st}_{0},p^{un}_{0})=(p^{st}_{-},p^{un}_{-})
and
(p^{st}_{k_{1}+1},p^{un}_{k_{1}+1})=(p^{st}_{+},p^{un}_{+})
;

•

for every
0\leq k\leq k_{1}
,
P_{k}^{st}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{k}^{st},p_{k+1}^{st};\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{st})
and
P_{k}^{un}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{k}^{un},p_{k+1}^{un};\mathfrak{a}_{m}^{un})
are some instantons; one of them must be non-constant in time;

•

for any
n
, there exists a sequence of numbers:

t^{0}_{n}<t^{1}_{n}<\cdots<t^{k_{1}}_{n}

such that for all
0\leq k\leq k_{1}
, we have

\tau_{t^{k}_{n}}P_{n}\to P_{k}^{un}\text{ and }(\tau_{t^{k}_{n}}P_{n})(\cdot,\cdot-R_{n})\to P_{k}^{st}

in
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-topology, and in addition
|t^{k+1}_{n}-t^{k}_{n}|\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

This lemma follows from Lemma
11.6
&
11.7
and the argument in
[
KM07
, Section 16.1& 16.2]
. Note that the divergence
|t^{k+1}_{n}-t^{k}_{n}|\to\infty
may be slower or faster than
R_{n}\to\infty
; they are not necessarily related.
∎

\begin{overpic}[Pic59.png] \put(12.0,73.0){$t_{n}^{0}$} \put(52.0,73.0){$t_{n}^{1}$}
\put(72.0,73.0){$t_{n}^{2}$}
\put(11.0,17.0){$P_{0}^{un}$}
\put(11.0,53.0){$P_{0}^{st}$} \put(51.0,17.0){$P_{1}^{un}$}
\put(51.0,53.0){$P_{1}^{st}$} \put(71.0,17.0){$P_{2}^{un}$}
\put(71.0,53.0){$P_{2}^{st}$} \put(30.0,65.0){$x_{1}$} \put(30.0,35.0){$x_{l}$} \put(30.0,5.0){$x_{m}$}
\put(0.0,17.0){$p_{0}^{un}$}
\put(0.0,53.0){$p_{0}^{st}$}
\put(30.0,17.0){$p_{1}^{un}$}
\put(30.0,53.0){$p_{1}^{st}$}
\put(62.0,17.0){$p_{2}^{un}$}
\put(62.0,53.0){$p_{2}^{st}$} \put(85.0,17.0){$p_{3}^{un}$}
\put(85.0,53.0){$p_{3}^{st}$} \end{overpic}

Figure 38
.
Converging to a broken instanton (
k_{1}=2
).

By Lemma
11.2
, for such a broken instanton (
11.18
), we must have

\displaystyle\gr(p_{R_{n},-})-\gr(p_{R_{n},+})

\displaystyle=\big(\gr(p^{st}_{0})-\gr(p^{st}_{k_{1}+1})\big)+\big(\gr(p^{un}_{0})-\gr(p^{un}_{k_{1}+1})\big)

(11.19)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{k_{1}}\big(\gr(p^{st}_{k})-\gr(p^{st}_{k+1})\big)+\big(\gr(p^{un}_{k})-\gr(p^{un}_{k+1})\big).

In the second line, each term in the summation is either the index of
P_{k}^{st}
or that of
P_{k}^{un}
. Since the Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{st}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{m}^{un}
are assumed to be admissible, each of them must be
\geq 0
and
=0
only if the associated instanton is a constant trajectory in time. Thus in the case that
\gr(p_{R,-})-\gr(p_{R,+})=1
, we must have
k_{1}=0
and

(11.20)

\big(\gr(p_{-}^{st})-\gr(p_{+}^{st}),\ \gr(p_{-}^{un})-\gr(p_{+}^{un})\big)=(0,1)\text{ or }(1,0)

depending on whether
P_{0}^{st}
or
P_{0}^{un}
is a constant trajectory; this explains the trichotomy in (
11.5
). In particular, we have proved that

Corollary 11.9
.

Suppose that
\big(\gr(p_{-}^{st})-\gr(p_{+}^{st})\big)+\big(\gr(p_{-}^{un})-\gr(p_{+}^{un})\big)=1
, then the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(p_{R,-},p_{R,+};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1})=\emptyset
for all
R\gg\pi
unless

(
i
)

\big(\gr(p_{-}^{st})-\gr(p_{+}^{st}),\ \gr(p_{-}^{un})-\gr(p_{+}^{un})\big)=(0,1)
and
p_{-}^{st}=p_{+}^{st}
; or

(
ii
)

\big(\gr(p_{-}^{st})-\gr(p_{+}^{st}),\ \gr(p_{-}^{un})-\gr(p_{+}^{un})\big)=(1,0)
and
p_{-}^{un}=p_{+}^{un}
.

Since the two cases in Corollary
11.9
are symmetric, we shall focus on the first one from now on. In this special case, Lemma
11.8
is refined as follows.

Corollary 11.10
.

Suppose that the first case of Corollary
11.9
happens. For any sequence of instantons
P_{n}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{R_{n},-},p_{R_{n},+};\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R_{n}})
with
R_{n}\to\infty
, there exists a
(
non-constant
)

\alpha^{un}_{m}
-instanton
P^{un}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{-}^{un},p_{+}^{un};\mathfrak{a}^{un}_{m})
and a sequence of real numbers
t^{n}
such that

\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}\to P^{un}\text{ and }(\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n})(\cdot,\cdot-R_{n})\to P^{st}

in
C^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
-topology, where
P^{st}
is the constant trajectory at
p^{st}_{-}=p^{st}_{+}
.

The
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-convergence in Corollary
11.10
is not quite enough for the proof of Theorem
11.3
. A global estimate in the spirit of (
11.10
) is more relevant. The proof of Lemma
11.8
implies the following stronger result.

Corollary 11.11
.

Then there exists a constant
C_{7},K_{7}>0
with the following properties. For the converging subsequence in Corollary
11.10
, we have

\|\grad\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(P_{R}(t,\cdot))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}^{2}<\frac{\epsilon_{5}}{4},

for all
|t|\geq K_{7}
and
R\gg\pi
; so the condition (
11.15
) holds on
I_{t}
for all
t>K_{7}+1
. Then (
11.17
) implies that

0\leq\mp\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p_{R,\pm})\pm\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(P_{R}(t,\cdot))\leq C_{7}e^{\pm C_{5}t},\ \forall\pm t\geq K_{7}+1.

Combined with (
11.16
), this means that the
L^{r}_{1}
-norm of
P_{R}-p_{R,\pm}
on
I_{t}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}
decays exponentially to zero as
\pm t\to\infty
. These estimates are uniform in the stretching parameter
R
and hold also for the limiting instanton
P^{un}\in\mathcal{M}(p^{un}_{-},p^{un}_{+};\mathfrak{a}^{un}_{m})
in Corollary
11.10
.

11.5.
Proof of Theorem
11.3
Part III: A gluing scheme

First recall the Implicit Function Theorem we shall use later:

Theorem 11.12

(
[
MS12
, Theorem A.3.3]
)
.

Let
X
and
Y
be Banach spaces and
U\subset X
be an open subset. If
F:U\to Y
is Fredholm
(
i.e. the linear map
dF(x):X\to Y
is bounded and Fredholm for all
x\in X)
and continuously differentiable, and
y
is a regular value of
F
, then
\mathcal{N}=F^{-1}(y)\subset U
is a
C^{1}
-manifold with
T_{x}\mathcal{N}=\ker dF(x)
for all
x\in\mathcal{N}
.

In what follows, we shall apply this theorem to a family of Fredholm maps
F:X\to Y
which depend on the stretching parameter
R
. The next proposition, which is used in the proof of Theorem
11.12
, becomes more useful in order to obtain estimates uniform in
R
.

Proposition 11.13

(
[
MS12
, Proposition A.3.4]
)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
11.12
, suppose that the differential
D=dF(x_{0}):X\to Y
is surjective at some
x_{0}\in U
and has a right inverse
Q:Y\to X
. Choose
c,\delta>0
such that
\|Q\|\leq c
and
\|dF(x)-D\|\leq\frac{1}{2c}
for all
x\in B(x_{0},\delta)\subset U
. If
x_{1}\in X
satisfies

\|F(x_{1})\|<\frac{\delta}{4c},\ \|x_{1}-x_{0}\|<\frac{\delta}{8},

then there exists a unique
x\in B(x_{0},\delta)
such that
F(x)=0
and
x-x_{1}\in\im Q
. Moreover,
\|x-x_{1}\|\leq 2c\|f(x_{1})\|
.

In Proposition
11.13
, one should think of
x_{0}
as a reference point and
x_{1}
as the approximate solution.

We begin with a linear problem to explain our gluing scheme. Suppose that
V
is a finite dimensional Euclidean space,
\sigma_{\pm}:V\to V
are self-adjoint and invertible, and
\sigma_{t}:V\to V,t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}
is a smooth family of self-adjoint operators such that
\|\sigma_{t}-\sigma_{\pm}\|_{L^{r}(I_{t},\End(V))}\to 0
,
I_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[t-1,t+1]
exponentially as
t\to\pm\infty
for some
r>2
. Then the linear map:

\displaystyle D:C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{s};V)

\displaystyle\to C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{s};V)

\displaystyle v(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto\partial_{t}v(t)+\sigma_{t}(v(t)).

is Fredholm from
L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t};V)
to
L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{t};V)
. If
D:L^{r}_{1}\to L^{r}
is invertible, then any
\xi\in L^{r}
has a unique solution
v\in L^{r}_{1}
such that
D(v)=\xi
.

However, we would like to solve this equation for
\xi
in a larger subspace of
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{s};\mathcal{V})
. Let
\chi_{\pm}:\mathbb{R}_{t}\to[0,1]
be a pair of cutoff functions such that for some
K>1
,

(11.21)

\chi_{-}(t)=\chi_{+}(-t),\ \chi_{+}(t)\equiv 1\text{ if }t\geq K+1\text{ and }\equiv 0\text{ if }t\leq K.

Let
\mathcal{V}_{j},j=0,1
denote the subspace of
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{s};V)
whose elements take the form

(11.22)

v=v^{-}\chi_{-}(s)+v^{+}\chi_{+}(s)+v_{0}\text{ with }v_{\pm}\in V_{\pm},v_{0}\in L^{r}_{j}(\mathbb{R}_{s};V)

where
V^{\pm}
is another copy of
V
, and
\mathcal{V}_{j}
is equipped with the norm on
V_{-}\oplus V_{+}\oplus L^{r}_{j}(\mathbb{R}_{s};V)
. Then
D
extends to a bounded operator
\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
which takes the form

(11.23)

\begin{pmatrix}v^{-}\\
v^{+}\\
v_{0}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{-}&0&0\\
0&\sigma_{+}&0\\
\partial_{t}\chi_{-}+\chi_{-}(\sigma_{t}-\sigma_{-})&\partial_{t}\chi_{+}+\chi_{+}(\sigma_{t}-\sigma_{+})&\partial_{t}+\sigma_{t}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}v^{-}\\
v^{+}\\
v_{0}\end{pmatrix}.

Since all diagonal entries are invertible, so is the operator
D:\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
. Thus any
\xi\in\mathcal{V}_{0}
also admit a unique solution
D(v)=\xi
with
v\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
. One should think of this
D
as the differential map
dF(x_{1})
in Proposition
11.13
and
\xi=F(x_{1})
is the error term to be corrected.

Returning to the proof of Theorem
11.3
, we fix some
r>2
from now on. suppose that we are in the first case of Corollary
11.9
,
P^{un}\in\mathcal{M}(p_{-}^{un},p_{+}^{un};\mathfrak{a}^{un}_{m})
is any
\alpha^{un}_{m}
-instanton, and
P^{st}
is the constant trajectory at
p^{st}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p^{st}_{-}=p^{un}_{+}
. For any
R\gg\pi
, we have to construct an instanton
P_{R}\in\mathcal{M}(p^{-}_{R},p^{+}_{R};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1})
such that
P_{R}\to(P^{un},P^{st})
as
R\to\infty
in the sense of Corollary
11.10
.

The approximate solution
P^{\pre}_{R}
is simply obtained by applying the pre-gluing map (
11.7
) to
P^{st}
and
P^{un}
at each time slice, i.e.,

(11.24)

P^{\pre}_{R}(t,\cdot)=\Phi_{R}(P^{st}(t,\cdot),P^{un}(t,\cdot))=\Phi_{R}(p^{st},P^{un}(t,\cdot)),\forall t\in\mathbb{R}_{t}.

It is also convenient to choose a reference trajectory which is constant when
|t|\gg 1
so that our gluing scheme looks more similar to one above. Consider an approximation
P^{un}_{K}
of
P^{un}
with
K\gg 1
such that

•

P^{un}_{K}(t,s)=P^{un}(t,s)
when
|t|\leq K-1
;

•

P^{un}_{K}(t,s)=p^{un}_{\pm}(s)
when
\pm t\geq K
;

•

\dist_{C^{0}}(P^{un},P^{un}_{K})<r_{0}
; identify
P^{un}
as a section of
L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s};(P^{un}_{K})^{*}TM)
, then

(11.25)

\delta_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\|P^{un}-P^{un}_{K}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s};(P^{un}_{K})^{*}TM)}<Ce^{-C_{5}K}

for some
C>0
, and
C_{5}
is the constant in Lemma
11.7
and Corollary
11.11
.

This approximation
P^{un}_{K}
is constructed using the exponential maps along
p^{un}_{\pm}
and some cutoff functions as in the case of the pre-gluing map (
11.7
), and the required exponential decay follows from Corollary
11.11
. Now we replace
P^{un}
by
P^{un}_{K}
in (
11.24
) to obtain a reference trajectory denoted by
P^{\refe}_{R}
, which satisfies the following properties:

•

P^{\refe}_{R}(t,\cdot)\equiv p_{R,\pm}^{\pre}=\Phi_{R}(p^{st},p^{un}_{\pm})
when
\pm t\geq K
;

•

P^{\refe}_{R}=P^{\pre}_{R}\equiv x_{l}
when
s\in[\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8},\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s}
;

•

P^{\refe}_{R}=P^{\pre}_{R}
when
|t|\leq K-1
.

Neither of
P^{\pre}_{R}
and
P^{\refe}_{R}
satisfy the boundary condition (
2.13
) yet, and they must be corrected as in our model problem above. Let
V^{\pm}_{j}=L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{R,\pm})^{*}TM)
and
V_{j}^{0}=L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s};(P^{\refe}_{R})^{*}TM),j=0,1.
Then define

\mathcal{V}_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}V^{-}_{j}\oplus V^{+}_{j}\oplus V^{0}_{j},\ j=0,1.

Since
P^{\refe}_{R}
is a constant trajectory when
|t|\geq K
, we can identify
\mathcal{V}_{j}
as a subspace of
L^{r}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s};(P^{\refe}_{R})^{*}TM)
using the cutoff functions (
11.21
) and the formula (
11.22
).

For any
x\in M
and
w\in T_{x}M
, let
\Psi_{x}(w):T_{x}M\to T_{\exp_{x}(w)}M
denote the parallel transportation along the geodesic
t\mapsto\exp_{x}(tw),t\in[0,1]
. For any
v\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
, consider the map
P_{v}(z)=\exp_{P^{\refe}_{R}(z)}(v(z)),z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Using the Floer datum
\mathfrak{a}_{m1}^{R}
, the formula (
5.5
) defines a section
\mathfrak{F}(P_{v}(z))\in L^{r}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s};(P_{v})^{*}TM\otimes\Lambda^{0,1}\mathbb{C})
,
\mathbb{C}_{z}=\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}
, which is turned into a non-linear map using the parallel transportation
\Psi
:

(11.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{1}

\displaystyle\to L^{r}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s};(P^{\refe}_{R})^{*}TM)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto\Psi_{P^{\refe}_{R}(z)}(v(z))^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{F}(P_{v}(z))\big).

where the bundle
\Lambda^{0,1}\mathbb{C}
has been trivialized using
d\overline{z}=dt-is
. Although we have dropped the subscript,
\mathcal{F}
depends on the stretching parameter
R\gg\pi
.

Suppose that under the exponential map, the approximate solution
P^{\pre}_{R}
is represented by the section
v_{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
, so
P^{\pre}_{R}(z)=P_{v_{1}}(z)=\exp_{P^{\refe}_{R}(z)}(v_{1}(z))
. Then in fact
v_{1}\in L^{r}_{1}
and
v_{1}\equiv 0
when
|t|\leq K-1
. To apply Proposition
11.13
to the non-linear map (
11.26
), we take
x_{0}=0\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
as the reference point and
x_{1}=v_{1}
as the approximate solution. The conditions of Proposition
11.13
are verified by the next lemma.

Lemma 11.14
.

There exists constants
\delta_{1},c,c_{1}>0
independent of
R,K
so that the following holds for
R\gg\pi
:

(
i
)

\mathcal{F}(0)\in\mathcal{V}_{0}
and
(d\mathcal{F})(0):\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
is bounded linear and Fredholm of index
1
;

(
ii
)

for all
v_{2},v_{3}\in B(0,\delta_{1})\subset\mathcal{V}_{1}
,

\|d\mathcal{F}(v_{2})-d\mathcal{F}(v_{3})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}}\leq c_{1}\|v_{2}-v_{3}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}.

Combined with the first property, this implies that the image of
\mathcal{F}(B(0,\delta_{1}))
lies in the smaller space
\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset L^{r}_{loc}
, and
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
is continuously differentiable;

(
iii
)

\|v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}}=\|v_{1}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\leq 2\delta_{K}
and
\|\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\to 0
as
R\to\infty
, with
\delta_{K}
defined as in (
11.25
);

(
iv
)

d\mathcal{F}(v_{1}):\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
is surjective and has a right inverse
Q_{R}
with norm bounded by
c
.

Take
F=\mathcal{F}
,
x_{0}=0
,
x_{1}=v_{1}
and and
\delta=\min\{\delta_{1},\frac{1}{2cc_{1}}\}
in Proposition
11.13
. We have to verify in addition that

\|\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}<\frac{\delta}{4c},\ \|v_{1}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}<\frac{\delta}{8}.

By Lemma
11.14
, this can be arranged by taking a fixed
K\gg 1
(use (
11.25
)) then by letting
R\to\infty
. Thus we obtain an
\alpha^{R}_{m1}
-instanton
P_{R}^{\star}(z)=\exp_{P^{\pre}_{R}(z)}(v_{\star}(z))\in\mathcal{M}(p^{R}_{-},p^{R}_{+};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1})
with
v_{\star}-v_{1}\in\im Q_{R}
and
\|v_{\star}-v_{1}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\leq 2c\|\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\to 0
as
R\to\infty
.

By Theorem
11.12
,
v_{R}
fits into a 1-parameter family of perturbed
\alpha^{R}_{m1}
-instantons. The uniqueness part of Proposition
11.13
then says that in a small neighborhood of
P_{R}^{\pre}
, this family is obtained by translating
P_{R}^{*}
. In the meantime, the
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-convergence in Corollary
11.10
can be improved as follows.

Lemma 11.15
.

For the converging subsequence in Corollary
11.10
, we have

\|\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}-P^{\pre}_{R_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
, where
\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}
,
P^{\pre}_{R_{n}}
are viewed as sections in
\mathcal{V}_{1}
using the exponential maps. This implies that
\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}
is a translated copy of
P_{R_{n}}^{\star}
.

Thus for
R\gg\pi
, any instanton in
\mathcal{M}(p^{R}_{-},p^{R}_{+};\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1})
is obtained up to a translation by gluing some
P^{un}
vertically with
P^{st}
. To complete the proof of Theorem
11.3
, it remains to prove Lemma
11.14
and
11.15
.

Proof of Lemma
11.14

(

i

)

.

For the reference trajectory
P^{\refe}_{R}
, the section
\mathcal{F}(0)
is defined by the formula

\partial_{t}P^{\refe}_{R}+J\partial_{s}P^{\refe}_{R}+\nabla\im(e^{-i\alpha_{m1}^{R}(s)}W)+\nabla\delta H^{m1}_{R}=0,

so
\mathcal{F}(0)(t,\cdot)\equiv\grad p^{\pre}_{R,\pm}\in L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{R,\pm})^{*}TM)
for
\pm t\geq K
. The gradient vector
\grad p^{\pre}_{R,\pm}
is supported on
[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{4}]_{s}
. By construction the difference

\mathcal{F}(0)-\chi_{-}(s)\grad p^{\pre}_{R,-}-\chi_{+}(s)\grad p^{\pre}_{R,+}.

is supported on

\{K-1\leq|t|\leq K+1\}\ \bigcup\ [-K-1,-K+1]_{t}\times[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{4}]_{s}

and lies in
L^{1}_{r}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
. The linearization of
\mathcal{F}
at
0
takes a lower triangular form as in (
11.23
):

(11.27)

\begin{pmatrix}\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p_{R,-}^{\pre})&0&0\\
0&\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p_{R,+}^{\pre})&0\\
\partial_{t}\chi_{-}&\partial_{t}\chi_{+}&D_{0}\end{pmatrix}

where
D_{0}
is
d\mathcal{F}(0)
as a map between
L^{r}_{1}\to L^{r}
. These entries are bounded linear, and so is
d\mathcal{F}(0):\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
. The Fredholm index will be computed later using
d\mathcal{F}(v_{1})
. ∎

Proof of Lemma

(

ii

)

.

The second statement

(

ii

)

requires some work. Its predecessor can be found in
[
Flo88b
, Theorem 3a]
,
[
AD14
, Lemma 9.4.8]
and
[
MS12
, Proposition 3.5.3]
, and we follow the same argument.The key ingredient is the following pointwise estimate.

Lemma 11.16
.

Let
S
be any Riemann surface,
M_{0}\subset M
any compact subset, and
P:S\to M_{0}\subset M
any smooth map. Following the construction of (
11.26
), for any smooth vector field
\mathcal{X}\in\Omega^{1}(S;C^{\infty}(M;TM))
, one can define a non-linear map

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{0}:C^{\infty}(S;P^{*}(TM))

\displaystyle\to C^{\infty}(S;P^{*}(TM)\otimes\Lambda^{0,1}S)

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto\Psi_{P}(\xi)^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\exp_{P}(\xi))\big)

with
\mathfrak{F}_{0}(P_{\xi})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(dP_{\xi}-\mathcal{X})^{0,1}
,
P_{\xi}=\exp_{P}(\xi)
. Then for any
c_{2}>0
, there exist some constant
C>0
such that for any smooth
\delta\xi_{1},\delta\xi_{2},\xi_{0},\xi\in C^{\infty}(B(0,1);P^{*}TM)
with
L^{\infty}
-norms
<c_{2}
, there are pointwise bounds

(11.28)

|(d\mathcal{F}_{0})(\xi_{0})\xi-(d\mathcal{F}_{0})(\xi_{0}+\delta\xi_{1})\xi|<C(|\delta\xi_{1}||\nabla\xi|+|\nabla\delta\xi_{1}||\xi|+|\delta\xi_{1}||\xi|),

and

(11.29)

\displaystyle\big|(d\mathcal{F}_{0})(\xi_{0})\xi-(d\mathcal{F}_{0})(\xi_{0}+\delta\xi_{1})\xi-(d\mathcal{F}_{0})(\xi_{0}+\delta\xi_{2})\xi+(d\mathcal{F}_{0})(\xi_{0}+\delta\xi_{2}+\delta\xi_{1})\xi\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|\delta\xi_{1}||\delta\xi_{2}||\nabla\xi|+|\nabla\delta\xi_{1}||\delta\xi_{2}||\xi|+|\nabla\delta\xi_{1}||\delta\xi_{2}||\xi|+|\delta\xi_{1}||\delta\xi_{2}||\xi|\big).

on
B(0,\frac{1}{2})
. This constant
C
depends on the geometry of
M
,
c_{2}>0
,
\|dP\|_{\infty}
and the
C^{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{X}
on a slightly larger compact subset of
M
depending on
c_{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
11.16
.

If one of
\xi_{0},\xi_{1}
is zero, then (
11.28
) follows from the computation in
[
MS12
, Proposition 3.5.3]
. In general,
(
11.30
)
and (
11.28
) follow from a (well-known) estimate on the second and the third derivatives of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
. For the sake of completeness, this estimate is summarized and proved in Lemma
B.1
.
∎

To apply Lemma
11.16
, let
P=P^{\pre}_{R}
and
\mathcal{X}
is the Hamiltonian vector field associated to
\im(e^{-\alpha_{m1}^{R}(s)}W)ds+\delta H_{m1}^{R}\in\Omega^{1}(S;C^{\infty}(M;\mathbb{R}))
. The compactness theorem implies that
P^{\pre}_{R}
lies a fixed compact subset of
M
. The
L^{\infty}_{1}
-norm of
P^{\pre}_{R}
and the
C^{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{X}
on this compact subset are uniformly bounded.

Let
\delta v=v_{3}-v_{2}
. We have to show that for all
v_{2},v_{3}\in B(0,\delta_{1})\subset\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
v\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
, the estimate

(11.30)

\|(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}+\delta v)(v)-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2})(v)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\leq c_{1}\|\delta v\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}

holds for some
c_{1}>0
. By the nature of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
, decompose
\delta v
as
\chi_{-}\delta v^{-}+\chi_{+}\delta v^{+}+\delta v^{0}
. It suffices to verify (
11.30
) when only one of
\delta v^{\pm},\delta v^{0}
is non-zero. Similarly write
v=\chi_{-}v^{-}+\chi_{+}v^{+}+v^{0}
with
v^{\pm}\in V_{1}^{\pm}
an
v^{0}\in V_{1}^{0}.

Case
1. If
\delta v=\delta v^{0}\in L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
, then we can prove a stronger result:

\|(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}+\delta v)(v)-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2})(v)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq c_{1}\|\delta v\|_{L^{r}_{1}}\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}.

This follows immediately from (
11.28
) by noting that
\mathcal{V}_{1}\subset L^{\infty}
, and

\nabla(\chi_{-}v^{-}+\chi_{+}v^{+})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{s}))\text{ and }L^{r}_{1}\hookrightarrow L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s}))\hookrightarrow L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{s})).

The multiplication
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{s}))\times L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{s}))\to L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is continuous. Thus

\displaystyle\|(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}+\delta v)(v)-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2})(v)\|_{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\||\delta v||\nabla v^{0}|\|_{r}+\||\delta v||\nabla(\chi_{-}v^{-}+\chi_{+}v^{+})|\|_{r}+\||\nabla\delta v||v|\|_{r}+\||\delta v||v|\|_{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\delta v\|_{\infty}\|\nabla v^{0}\|_{r}+\|\delta v\|_{L^{r}(L^{\infty})}\|\nabla(\chi_{-}v^{-}+\chi_{+}v^{+})\|_{L^{\infty}(L^{r})}+\|\nabla\delta v\|_{r}\|v\|_{\infty}+\||\delta v\|_{r}\|v\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\delta v\|_{L^{r}_{1}}\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}.

Case
2. If
\delta v=\chi_{-}\delta v^{-}
, then we think of
(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}+\delta v)-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2})
as a lower triangular matrix as in (
11.23
) and verify that each component is bounded. The same argument as in
Case
1 shows that this operator is
L^{r}_{1}\to L^{r}
and it acts trivially on
v=\chi_{+}v^{+}
,
v^{+}\in V_{1}^{+}
.

We focus on the case when
v=\chi_{-}v^{-}
for some
v^{-}\in V^{-}_{1}
. The
(1,1)
-entry of (
11.23
) is bounded using Lemma
11.16
(this is a property about the real line
\mathbb{R}_{s}
). Since the section
v
is supported on
\{t\leq-K\}
, we may pretend that
P^{\refe}_{R}
is the constant trajectory at
p^{\pre}_{R,-}
and
v_{2}=\chi_{-}v_{2}^{-}+v_{2}^{0}
for this computation. With this understood, the
(3,1)
-entry of (
11.23
) for
(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}+\delta v)-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2})
applied to
v=\chi_{-}v^{-}
is given by

(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}+\chi_{-}\delta v^{-})(\chi_{-}v^{-})-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2})(\chi_{-}v^{-})-\chi_{-}\big((d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}^{-}+\delta v^{-})(v^{-})-(d\mathcal{F})(v_{2}^{-})(v^{-})\big)

which is bounded pointwise by

\chi_{-}(|\delta v^{-}||v^{-}||v_{2}^{0}|+|\nabla\delta v^{-}||v^{-}||v_{2}^{0}|+|\delta v^{-}||\nabla v^{-}||v_{2}^{0}|+|\delta v^{-}||v^{-}||\nabla v_{2}^{0}|)

up to a uniform constant. This is due to (
11.29
). Since
\nabla\delta v^{-},\nabla v^{-}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{s}))
and
v_{2}^{0}\in L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})\hookrightarrow L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{t};L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s}))
, we conclude that this
(3,1)
-entry applied to
v=\chi_{-}v^{-}
has
L^{r}
-norm bounded by

\|v^{0}_{2}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})}\|\delta v^{-}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}\|v^{-}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}.

Case
3. The case that
\delta v=\chi_{-}\delta^{+}
is identical to
Case
2.

This completes the proof of Lemma
11.14

(

ii

)

.
∎

Proof of Lemma
11.14

(

iii

)

.

Note that if
s\leq\frac{(R+\pi)}{4}
, then
P^{\pre}_{R}=P^{un}
and
P^{\refe}_{R}=P^{un}_{K}
, so
v_{1}
is equal to the section
P^{un}-P^{un}_{K}
in (
11.25
). Moreover,
v_{1}\equiv 0
if
s\geq\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8}
. For
s\in[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s}
, since the instanton
P^{un}
decays exponentially to
x_{l}
as
s\to\infty
, one verifies that

\|v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s})}\lesssim\|P^{un}-P^{un}_{K}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s})}\to 0

as
R\to\infty
. This implies that
\|v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}}\leq 2\delta_{K}
for
R\gg\pi
.

To estimate
\|\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}
, since
\Psi_{P^{\refe}_{R}}(v_{1})
is an isometry, it suffices to estimate
\mathfrak{F}(P^{\pre}_{R})
. This section is supported on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{4}]_{s}
. Apply Proposition
3.6
to
P^{un}
and
P^{st}
; we conclude that the
L^{r}_{1}
-norm of
\mathfrak{F}(P^{\pre}_{R})
on
[-R,R]_{t}\times[\frac{R+\pi}{4},\frac{3(R+\pi)}{4}]_{s}
decays exponentially as
R\to\infty
. The limits of
\mathfrak{F}(P^{\pre}_{R})
at
\pm\infty
are given precisely by
\grad\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p^{\pre}_{R,\pm})
which also decays exponentially as
R\to\infty
in
L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s})
. Finally, the convergence
P^{\pre}_{R}(t,\cdot)\to p^{\pre}_{R,\pm}
is also exponentially in
L^{r}_{1}(I_{t}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
as
t\to\pm\infty
, so
\mathfrak{F}(P^{\pre}_{R})-\grad\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p^{\pre}_{R,\pm})\to 0
exponentially in
L^{r}(I_{t}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}_{s})
. We conclude that
\|\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}\to 0
as
R\to\infty
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
11.14

(

iv

)

.

Let
D^{un},D^{st}
denote the linearization of the
\alpha
-instanton equation at
P^{st}
and
P^{un}
. Then
D^{st}
is invertible, and
\ker D^{un}
is 1-dimensional and is generated by
\partial_{t}P^{st}
. Choose a smooth section
v^{\prime}
of
(P^{un})^{*}TM\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
supported on
[-1,1]_{t}\times[-1,1]_{s}
such that the
L^{2}
-pairing
f=\langle\cdot,v^{\prime}\rangle
is non-zero on
\ker D^{un}
, so

D^{un}\oplus f:L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2};(P^{un})^{*}TM)\to L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2};(P^{un})^{*}TM\oplus\mathbb{R}.

is invertible. Such a section
v^{\prime}
exists due to a unique continuation property of
\partial_{t}P^{st}
. Since
P^{\refe}_{R}=P^{\pre}_{R}=P^{un}
on
[-1,1]_{t}\times[-1,1]_{s}
,
f
can be viewed also a map
\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
. We first consider the linearization of
\mathfrak{F}
at
P^{\pre}_{R}
:

D^{\pre}_{R}:L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2};(P^{\pre}_{R})^{*}TM)\to L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2};(P^{\pre}_{R})^{*}TM).

Since
P^{\pre}_{R}\equiv x_{l}
when
s\in[\frac{3(R+\pi)}{8},\frac{5(R+\pi)}{8}]_{s}
, the same excision argument using the transformation (
11.8
) on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}
allows us to compare
D^{st}\oplus D^{un}\oplus f
with the operator

(\partial_{t}+D_{x_{l}})\oplus(D^{\pre}_{R}\oplus f).

and obtain an equation similar to (
11.2
). Again the error term
\delta_{R}
has operator norm
\to 0
as
R\to\infty
, so
D^{\pre}_{R}\oplus f:L^{r}_{1}\to L^{r}
is also invertible, and its inverse is bounded uniformly. Returning to the reference trajectory
P^{\refe}_{R}
, this implies that
d\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\oplus f:L^{r}_{1}\to L^{r}
is invertible. As a map from
\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}
,
d\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\oplus f
is cast into a lower triangular matrix as in (
11.23
), and we just verified that the last diagonal entry is invertible. The first two are given by
\Hess\mathcal{A}_{W,\mathfrak{a}^{R}_{m1}}(p^{\pre}_{R,\pm})
, which are invertible by Lemma
11.5
. Off-diagonal entries are bounded by

(

ii

)

. This proves that
d\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\oplus f:\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{0}\oplus\mathbb{R}
is invertible. We take
Q_{R}=(d\mathcal{F}(v_{1})\oplus f)^{-1}|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}
. Then the operator norm of
Q_{R}
is bounded uniformly and
\im Q_{R}=\ker f
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
11.15
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, we prove that
\|\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}-P^{\pre}_{R_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}<100\epsilon
for
R_{n}\gg\pi
. The limits of
\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}
and
P^{\pre}_{R_{n}}
at
\pm\infty
are given respectively by
p_{R_{n},\pm}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p^{st}_{\pm}\circ_{R_{n}}p^{un}
and
p^{\pre}_{R_{n},\pm}
. Write

p^{st}_{\pm}\circ_{R_{n}}p^{un}=\exp_{p^{\pre}_{R_{n},\pm}}(v_{R_{n}}^{\pm})

with
v_{R_{n}}^{\pm}\in L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s};(p^{\pre}_{R_{n},\pm})^{*}TM)=V_{1}^{\pm}
, then by (
11.10
)

\|v_{R_{n}}^{\pm}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{s})}<\epsilon

for
R_{n}\gg\pi
. Write

\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}=\exp_{P^{\refe}_{R}}(v_{n}),\ v_{n}=\chi_{-}v_{R_{n}}^{-}+\chi_{+}v_{R_{n}}^{+}+v_{n}^{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}.

We have to show that
\|v_{n}^{0}-v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}}\to 0
for
R_{n}\gg\pi
. Recall that

P^{\pre}_{R_{n}}=\exp_{P^{\refe}_{R}}(v_{1}),\ v_{1}\in L^{r}_{1}=V_{1}^{0}.

Since the convergences
P^{\pre}_{R_{n}}\to p^{\pre}_{R_{n},\pm}
and
\tau_{t_{n}}P_{n}\to p_{R_{n},\pm}
are exponential as
t\to\pm\infty
(and uniform in
R_{n}
), for some
K_{\epsilon}\gg 0
, we have

\|v_{n}^{0}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\{|t|\geq K_{\epsilon}\})}<\epsilon,\ \|v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\{|t|\geq K_{\epsilon}\})}<\epsilon.

By Lemma
11.6
and Proposition
3.6
, for some
R_{\epsilon}>0
,

\|v_{n}^{0}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\{|s|>R_{\epsilon}\}\cap\{|s-R|\geq R_{\epsilon}\})}<\epsilon,\ \|v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\{|s|>R_{\epsilon}\}\cap\{|s-R|\geq R_{\epsilon}\})}<\epsilon.

Thus it suffices to estimate the
L^{r}_{1}
-norm of
|v_{n}^{0}-v_{1}|
on the rectangles

\Omega_{\epsilon}=[-K_{\epsilon},K_{\epsilon}]_{t}\times[-R_{\epsilon},R_{\epsilon}]_{s}\ \bigcup\ [-K_{\epsilon},K_{\epsilon}]_{t}\times[R-R_{\epsilon},R+R_{\epsilon}]_{s}.

However,
\|v_{n}^{0}-v_{1}\|_{L^{r}_{1}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}\to 0
as
R_{n}\to\infty
by the
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-convergence in Corollary
11.11
. This completes the proof of Lemma
11.15
.
∎

11.6.
Quasi-units revisited

Finally we prove Proposition
8.18
using the vertical gluing theorem. We focus on the second case when
q_{0}=q_{1}
and set up the neck-stretching picture. As we will see shortly, this case is identical to the one addressed in Theorem
11.4
. Since
l_{q_{2},\theta_{2}}
intersects with
l_{q_{0},\theta_{0}}
at
y_{0}^{\prime}
, we must have
\theta_{0}-\pi<\theta_{2}
. Choose another angle
\theta_{-1}=\theta_{0}+\epsilon
for some
0<\epsilon\ll 1
such that

(11.31)

\theta_{0}-\pi<\theta_{-1}-\pi<\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{0}<\theta_{-1}.

Moreover, if
l_{q_{2},\theta_{2}}
intersects with the ray
l_{q,\theta_{-1}}
at
y_{-1}^{\prime}
, then we require that the triangle
(y^{\prime}_{-1},y_{1}^{\prime},W(q))
contains no critical values of
W
in the interior (think of
l_{q,\theta_{-1}}
as a slight perturbation of
l_{q,\theta_{0}})
. Now consider the thimbles:

U_{\star}=\Lambda_{q,\theta_{-1}-\pi}\text{ and }S_{\star}=\Lambda_{q,\theta_{-1}}.

and write for convenience
\Lambda_{j}=\Lambda_{q_{j},\theta_{j}},j=0,1,2
.
For some
0<\delta\ll 1
, fix smooth monotone functions
\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{-1}-\pi&\text{if }s\geq\pi-\delta,\\
\theta_{j}-\pi&\text{if }s\leq\delta,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{2}&\text{if }s\geq\pi-\delta,\\
\theta_{-1}-\pi&\text{if }s\leq\delta.\end{array}\right.

Choose admissible Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{j}=(\pi,\alpha_{j}(s),\beta,\epsilon,\delta H_{j}\equiv 0)
for the pair
(\Lambda_{j},U_{\star})
,
j=0,1
and
\mathfrak{a}_{2}=(\pi,\alpha_{2}(s),\beta,\epsilon,\delta H_{2})
for
(S_{\star},\Lambda_{2})
. Then they can be concatenated as in Section
11.1
to give a Floer datum
\mathfrak{a}_{j2}^{R}=(R+\pi,\alpha_{j2}^{R}(s),\beta,\epsilon,\delta H_{j2}^{R})
for the pair
(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{2})
with
\alpha_{j2}^{R}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
defined similarly as in (
9.7
) and
\delta H_{j2}^{R}
as in (
11.3
) for all
R\geq\pi
and
j=0,1
. Finally, choose any admissible Floer data
\mathfrak{a}_{01}=(\pi,\alpha_{01},\beta_{01},\epsilon,\delta H_{01}\equiv 0)
for the pair
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1})
.

.

\alpha_{12}^{R}
-soliton. As

, it converges to the constant soliton at

followed by an

By (
11.31
), the function
\alpha_{j2}^{R}(s):\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
is also monotone for all
R\geq\pi
and
j=0,1
. Combined with our condition on the critical values of
W
, this implies that the complex

\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{j2}^{R}),j=0,1

only has one filtration level when
R\gg\pi
, and in fact

\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{j2}^{R})\cong\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(S_{\star},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{2})\otimes\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{j},U_{\star};\mathfrak{a}_{j}).

This means any soliton
p_{R}\in\mathfrak{C}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{j2}^{R})
is obtained by gluing some
p_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}(S_{\star}.\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{2})
with the constant soliton
e_{q}\in\mathfrak{C}(\Lambda_{j},U_{\star};\mathfrak{a}_{j})
. As in the case of Theorem
11.4
, the vertical gluing theorem in this case identifies the cobordism map

(11.36)

\mu^{2}:\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{12}^{R})\otimes\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1},\mathfrak{a}_{01})\to\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{02}^{R})

with the tensor product of the identity continuation map

\Id:\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(S_{\star},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{2})\to\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(S_{\star},\Lambda_{2};\mathfrak{a}_{2})

and the cobordism map

\mu^{2}_{q}:\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{1},U_{\star};\mathfrak{a}_{1})\otimes\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1},\mathfrak{a}_{01})\to\Ch_{\natural}^{*}(\Lambda_{0},U_{\star};\mathfrak{a}_{0}).

For the very last map
\mu^{2}_{q}
, all thimbles are associated to the same
q\in\Crit(W)
; so each Floer complex has rank 1 and is generated by the quasi-unit
e_{q}
. Then
\mu^{2}_{q}
is an isomorphism by Lemma
8.20
. This implies that the cobordism map
\mu^{2}
in(
11.36
) is a chain isomorphism for
R\gg\pi
. This completes the proof of Proposition
8.18
. One may compare Figure
40
below with Figure
36
.

11.7.
Seidel’s long exact sequence

Proposition
8.18
is concerned with the case when there is no critical values in interior of
(y_{0}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime},W(q))
, so the thimble
\Lambda_{q,\theta_{0}}
can be deformed continuous into
\Lambda_{q,\theta_{1}}
without violating the condition (
2.8
) or the weaker version in Remark
2.7
. But what if there is such a critical value?

\begin{overpic}[Pic61.png] \put(-20.0,20.0){$q=q_{0}=q_{1}$} \put(5.0,65.0){$q_{2}$}

\end{overpic}
